- Branch `claude/faz3a-bag`, tasks 1–7 complete and reviewed, Task 8 implemented (`9356f88`) and now under review.
- I verified against the **live** database (the Supabase MCP server is connected this session, so the handoff note's "no way to run SQL remotely" limitation no longer applies): all 48 migrations are applied remotely, and the two conversion paths the note flagged — `check_inden_ayril` and the pg_cron job — carry byte-identical narrowing logic. That risk is clean.
- But there's a **third** conversion path the phase's own comments don't acknowledge: `check_in_yap` closes your previous active check-in with `set konum = null` and never touches `gorunurluk`. Since `gorunurluk` defaults to `herkese_acik`, a `gizli` check-in that gets superseded by a new one becomes a publicly visible memory. It's inherited from Faz 2b, but Task 8 rewrote that function, so it sits inside this diff.

Waiting on the reviewer's verdict before ruling on it.

<result>### Spec Compliance

✅ Spec compliant. The delivered migration (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190832_check_in_yap_bulunurluk.sql`) and the sema-dogrula.ts test addition are byte-for-byte identical to the SQL/TS specified in the task brief (`.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-19-faz3a-bag/task-8-brief.md:21-150`) and match the plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-19-faz3a-bag.md:841-966`) exactly. `check_in_yap`'s new signature matches what Task 11 is documented to consume, and the dropped old signature (`uuid, double precision, double precision, text, text, boolean`) exactly matches the live function it replaces (confirmed against `20260816170000_check_in_yap_gizli.sql:3-10`).

⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether `npm run test:sema` genuinely printed "31 test PASSED" and whether `test:gorunurluk`'s 10 failures are exactly the ones attributable to the old `p_gizli_mi` parameter — I did not re-run the suites (per instructions), but I did verify against `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-19-faz3a-bag.md:552-556` that dropping `test:gorunurluk` scenarios at this point (pending Task 17's rewrite) is explicitly the plan's expected/endorsed behavior, not implementer improvisation. The controller should spot-check the actual `npm run test:sema` output for a clean "31/31" pass if that evidence matters.

### Named-risk verification (controller-flagged)

Checked: whether the "gorunurluk never widens" CASE expression is identical between `check_inden_ayril` (lines 77-87) and the pg_cron job body (lines 96-103) in `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190832_check_in_yap_bulunurluk.sql`. Result: **identical, character-for-character** — no divergence between the two paths the controller asked about.

However, checking this invariant more broadly surfaced a **third** silent conversion path the controller didn't name, inside the very function this diff rewrites (see Critical finding below).

### Strengths

- SQL matches brief/plan verbatim; no scope creep, no missing pieces relative to what was asked.
- Every `security definer` function follows the mandated shape: `set search_path = public`, `auth.uid() is null` guard as the first statement, and `revoke ... from public, anon; grant ... to authenticated;` at the end (`check_in_yap` lines 13-14, 21-23, 60-61; `check_inden_ayril` lines 69-70, 73-75, 90-91).
- No non-ASCII characters, no BOM (`od -An -tx1` on first 3 bytes → `2d 2d 20`, i.e. `-- `).
- New migration file, no edits to an already-applied migration.
- The two documented conversion paths (`check_inden_ayril`, cron) are kept in exact lockstep — the specific divergence risk the controller flagged does not materialize.
- Application-level `p_bulunurluk` validation (line 25-27) produces a friendly, test-observable Turkish error distinct from the raw DB constraint violation, and the new sema-dogrula.ts test correctly exercises it (invalid value is rejected before the mekan lookup, so the bogus `p_mekan_id` in the test doesn't interfere).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)

- **`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190832_check_in_yap_bulunurluk.sql:40-44`** — Third, unguarded check-in→memory conversion path that widens visibility. When a user calls `check_in_yap` while they already have an active (live) check-in elsewhere, the function force-closes the old row:
  ```sql
  update public.check_inler
  set konum = null
  where kullanici_id = auth.uid()
    and konum is not null
    and bitis_zamani &gt; now();
  ```
  Setting `konum = null` is exactly what makes a row a "memory" per the app's own RLS policy (`20260819185621_check_inler_bag_gorunurlugu.sql:16`: "Ani (konum bos): ani ekseni karar verir"). But unlike `check_inden_ayril` and the cron job, this UPDATE never touches `gorunurluk` — it leaves it at whatever it currently is, which for a freshly-inserted row is the column default `'herkese_acik'` (`20260815001911_check_inler.sql:10`). Concretely: a user checks in at place A with `bulunurluk = 'gizli'` (only they can see it live), then checks in at place B. Place A's row is silently converted to a memory with `gorunurluk` still `'herkese_acik'` — now visible to everyone, contradicting the very invariant this migration documents ("gorunurluk HICBIR ZAMAN genislemez", line 63) and that the controller specifically asked to verify. This is a real, silent privacy exposure of location data, which the project's own spec treats as its core risk ("core risk" section in CLAUDE.md). Fix: apply the same narrowing `case` used in `check_inden_ayril`/the cron job to this UPDATE as well (or factor it into a shared function/expression called from all three sites).
  - Note for the controller: this exact defect (a bare `konum = null` update with no visibility narrowing) already existed pre-Task-8 in `20260816170000_check_in_yap_gizli.sql:35-38`, from before the narrowing rule was even introduced (2026-08-17). Task 8 rewrites this entire function under the explicit "gorunurluk never widens" banner but reproduces the same gap rather than closing it — so it is squarely in the code delivered by this diff even though it isn't a new regression introduced by the implementer's own choices.

#### Important (Should Fix)

- **`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190832_check_in_yap_bulunurluk.sql:79-83` vs `:98-102`** (plan-mandated) — The narrowing `case` expression is hand-duplicated verbatim across `check_inden_ayril` and the pg_cron job body (a raw SQL string, so it can't call the plpgsql function directly). This is exactly the divergence risk the controller flagged; today the two copies match, but any future edit to one and not the other reintroduces the divergence silently, with no test that would catch it since `test:gorunurluk`'s narrowing scenarios are still tied to the old `gizli_mi` model until Task 17. This duplication comes from the brief itself, not an implementer decision, so it's reported as plan-mandated. Extracting a small `security definer` helper (e.g. `gizli.ani_donusum_gorunurlugu(p_bulunurluk text, p_gorunurluk text) returns text`) and calling it from both the RPC and the cron SQL would remove the duplication and the divergence risk in one move — and would also be the natural place to fix the Critical finding above by calling it from `check_in_yap`'s auto-close UPDATE too.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

- No test in this diff exercises the narrowing behavior itself (only the input-validation branch is tested here); full behavioral verification is deferred to Task 17's live `test:gorunurluk` suite. This matches the brief's scoped Step 2 exactly, so it's not a defect of this task, just worth the controller knowing coverage of the core invariant is not closed yet.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The implementer executed the brief precisely and the two named conversion paths (`check_inden_ayril`, cron) are kept in perfect sync, satisfying the controller's specific ask. But auditing the underlying invariant ("gorunurluk never widens on conversion") more broadly surfaced a genuine third conversion path — `check_in_yap`'s auto-close of a stale check-in — that silently resets visibility to the wide default, undermining the exact guarantee this migration is meant to establish; this should be closed (ideally alongside the duplicated-CASE cleanup) before this is trusted as "done."</result>

## Claude _2026-08-19 19:42:35 UTC_

Unutmuşum, kalıcı hafızaya da yazdım ki bir daha olmasın.

**Şu ana kadarki durum (Faz 3a, dal `claude/faz3a-bag`):**

Devam notunu ve yürütme defterini okudum. Görev 1-7 tamam ve incelenmiş; Görev 8 uygulanmış ama incelenmemişti — devam noktası orasıydı.

**Görev 8'in incelemesi bitti.** Spec uyumu ✅ (migrasyon brief'le birebir), ama kalite "düzeltme gerekiyor". İki bulgu çıktı:

1. **Kritik — üçüncü dönüşüm yolu.** Devam notu "gorunurluk asla genişlemez" kuralının **iki** yerde (`check_inden_ayril` RPC'si ve pg_cron işi) doğru yazılıp yazılmadığına bakmamı söylüyordu. İkisi de tertemiz, birebir aynı. Ama **üçüncü** bir dönüşüm yolu varmış ve orada daraltma hiç yok: `check_in_yap`, yeni check-in açmadan önce eski aktif check-in'i `set konum = null` ile kapatıyor, `gorunurluk`'e hiç dokunmuyor. `gorunurluk` sütununun varsayılanı `herkese_acik` olduğu için, `gizli` bir check-in yapıp başka bir yere check-in yapan kullanıcının eski kaydı **herkese görünür bir anı** olarak kalıyordu. Bu, fazın çekirdek kuralının ve projenin en baştan tanımladığı asıl riskin (yabancılarla konum paylaşımı) doğrudan ihlali.

2. **Önemli — kopyalanan mantık.** Daraltma `case` ifadesi elle iki kez yazılmış; üçüncü çağrı yeri eklenince üç kopya olacaktı.

Bu kusuru iki bağımsız yoldan doğruladım: bir yandan inceleyici diff üzerinden buldu, bir yandan ben canlı veritabanına doğrudan SQL çalıştırarak gördüm. İkisi aynı yere çıktı.

**Verdiğim iki karar** (defterde `Ruling:` olarak duruyor):
- Kritik bulgu düzeltilecek — pazarlık yok.
- Önemli bulgu da düzeltilecek: `case` ifadesi `bag.ani_gorunurlugu()` yardımcısına çıkarılıp üç yerden de çağrılacak. Plan iki kopyayı açıkça emrediyordu ama plan yalnızca **iki** dönüşüm yolu olduğunu varsayıyordu; üçüncüsü ortaya çıkınca planın gerekçesi çöküyor. Bağlayıcı otorite spec ve spec "görünürlük asla genişlemez" diyor.

Düzeltme alt ajana gönderildi, şu an çalışıyor. Sonucu gelince kapsamlı bir yeniden inceleme yapıp Görev 9'dan 18'e kadar devam edeceğim.

Bir de not: bu oturumda Supabase MCP bağlı, yani devam notundaki "uzak veritabanında serbest SQL çalıştırmanın yolu yok" tuzağı artık geçerli değil — doğrulamalar için elimde ikinci bir yol var. Bunu da deftere yazdım.

<result>### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant: the migration (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819194743_engelle_baglari_kaldir.sql:1-38`) matches the brief's proposed SQL essentially verbatim — same file name pattern, same body copied from `20260816150000_engelleme_rpc.sql:1-19` (auth guard first line, self-block check, insert), plus exactly the two `delete` statements the brief specified (lines 43-50 of the brief, lines 42-49 of the diff). `public.engeli_kaldir` left untouched. No existing migration edited — a new file was created. `revoke`/`grant authenticated` convention present at file end (diff lines 53-54). Confirmed independently: no BOM (`2d 2d 20` = `-- `), no non-ASCII bytes (`grep -nP '[^\x00-\x7F]'` → no matches).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether `git push` should have followed the commit. CLAUDE.md's current policy is "her sey push edilir" and the report states "No push was performed... commit locally only," but the task brief's Step 3 only instructs `git commit`, not push, so this may be handled by the orchestrator at a later stage rather than being this task's responsibility. Flagging for the controller to confirm.

### Named-risk verification

**Checked:** whether the two new deletes in `public.engelle` (`20260819194743_engelle_baglari_kaldir.sql:43-49`) preserve the recorded justification that "the sender cannot cancel their own request, so a scanner cannot decrement their own counter."

**Conclusion: the justification is violated — this is a concrete, working bypass of the 50/day cap.**

Evidence trail:
- `bag.istek_on_kontrol` (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql:27-33`) counts rows in `public.takipler` where `takip_eden_id = auth.uid()` and `public.sohbet_istekleri` where `gonderen_id = auth.uid()` — i.e. it counts *the sender's own outgoing rows*, live.
- The existing, deliberately asymmetric response RPCs enforce "only the recipient can delete a pending request": `public.takip_istegini_yanitla` deletes only `where takip_eden_id = p_kullanici_id and takip_edilen_id = auth.uid()` (`bag_rpcleri.sql:96-99`) — the caller (`auth.uid()`) must be the *recipient*, never the sender. Same pattern in `public.sohbet_istegini_yanitla` (`bag_rpcleri.sql:197-200`). This is exactly the mechanism the accepted ruling relies on.
- Task 9's new deletes in `public.engelle` are symmetric/bidirectional: `where (takip_eden_id = auth.uid() and takip_edilen_id = p_kullanici_id) or (takip_eden_id = p_kullanici_id and takip_edilen_id = auth.uid())` (diff lines 43-45), and the equivalent for `sohbet_istekleri` (diff lines 47-49). The first OR-branch matches rows where **`auth.uid()` is the sender** — i.e., `engelle` lets the original requester delete their own outgoing request, something no other RPC in the codebase permits.
- `public.engeli_kaldir` (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260816150000_engelleme_rpc.sql:21-34`) has no rate limit, no cooldown, and no precondition — anyone can call `engelle` then `engeli_kaldir` freely and repeatedly.

**Concrete exploit:** account A calls `takip_istegi_gonder(B)` (counter +1), immediately calls `engelle(B)` (deletes A's own just-inserted `takipler` row, counter back to prior value), optionally `engeli_kaldir(B)`. Repeating this against distinct targets C, D, E, ... lets A send an unbounded number of follow/chat requests per day while the counted total never grows. This is precisely the attack the cap's own code comment says it exists to stop: *"Kisi basi sinir yok (karar #37); bu tavan tek hesabin yuzlerce kisiye istek atarak dizini taramasini engelliyor"* (`bag_rpcleri.sql:35-36`) — "no per-person limit; this ceiling exists to stop a single account from scanning the directory by sending requests to hundreds of people." Task 9's deletes defeat that ceiling.

### Strengths
- Migration body is copied faithfully from the already-applied `20260816150000_engelleme_rpc.sql`, preserving the auth guard, self-block check, and security-definer conventions (`set search_path = public`, `auth.uid() is null` check first, trailing `revoke`/`grant`).
- Correction delivered as a new migration file rather than editing the applied one, per the immutability rule.
- Report's verification claims (BOM, ASCII, `revoke`/`grant`) are accurate and independently reproducible.
- Comment (diff lines 17-19) correctly documents the *intended* effect (closing the silent-block gap for existing follow relationships) — that specific goal is achieved correctly for the non-sender direction.

### Issues

#### Important (Should Fix) — plan-mandated
- **`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819194743_engelle_baglari_kaldir.sql:43-49`** — the bidirectional deletes in `public.engelle` let the sender of a pending follow/chat request delete their own outgoing row by blocking their own target, then optionally unblocking via the pre-existing, uncapped `public.engeli_kaldir` (`20260816150000_engelleme_rpc.sql:21-34`). Because `bag.istek_on_kontrol` (`20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql:27-33`) computes the daily cap by counting currently-existing rows, this reopens exactly the "scanner decrements their own counter" bypass that the accepted design ruling assumed was impossible, and defeats the cap's stated purpose of stopping directory-scanning via mass requests. This exactly matches what the brief itself specifies (brief lines 43-50), so the defect originates in the plan, not an implementer deviation — the implementer correctly and faithfully executed the brief. Fix requires a design change: e.g. split the delete so only the *recipient*-side row is removed by `engelle` (mirroring `takip_istegini_yanitla`'s asymmetry) while the sender's own pending row is left to expire/be counted, or move the daily cap to an append-only ledger unaffected by later deletes.

### Minor (Nice to Have)
- Report states "No push was performed, per instructions (commit locally only)" without citing which instruction overrides the current repo-wide push policy; worth the controller confirming this is intentional rather than a lapsed instruction.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The migration is implemented exactly as specified and passes all stated checks, but the brief's own design (faithfully implemented) reopens a rate-limit bypass on the daily request cap that the planning phase explicitly ruled out — this should go back to the design/plan level before being accepted, not be patched over silently at the task level.</result>

## Claude _2026-08-19 19:56:08 UTC_

**Görev 9'da gerçek bir tasarım kusuru çıktı ve karara bağladım.**

İnceleyici bağımsız olarak aynı sonuca vardı, üstelik istismarı benim tahminimden basit gösterdi: A, B'ye istek gönderir (sayaç +1), hemen B'yi engeller (kendi yeni satırı silinir, sayaç geri düşer), isterse engeli kaldırır. C, D, E ile tekrarlanınca günlük 50 tavanı tamamen etkisiz kalıyor — yani tavanın tek amacı olan "tek hesabın dizini taraması"nı engelleme işlevi yok oluyordu.

Spec'e baktım ve **iki tarafın da bağlayıcı** olduğunu gördüm:
- Spec açıkça "iki yöndeki takip ve sohbet kayıtları silinir" diyor → Görev 9'un çift yönlü `delete`'i doğru, geri alınamaz.
- Spec tavanın amacını da açıkça yazıyor → o da bağlayıcı.

**Kararım:** `delete`'ler aynen kalıyor, düzeltme **tavan tarafında** yapılıyor. Yeni bir `istek_gunlugu` ekle-only tablosu açılıyor; tavan artık canlı satırları değil bu günlüğü sayıyor; iki gönderme RPC'si başarılı gönderimde günlüğe bir satır yazıyor. Böylece hiçbir silme — ne engelleme, ne red — sayacı düşüremiyor.

Gerekçe: spec tavan için yalnızca "kalıbı `mekan_ekle`'nin günlük limitiyle aynı" diyor, "canlı satır say" demiyor. Ve `mekan_ekle`'nin saydığı `mekanlar` satırları hiç silinmiyor — yani kalıbın kendisi de zaten ekle-only. Tavan tarafını değiştirmek spec'e sadık kalan tek yol.

Yan fayda: planlama aşamasında bilerek kabul edilmiş bir zayıflık daha kapanıyor — alıcının reddi de sayacı düşürüyordu, artık düşürmüyor.

Düzeltme gönderildi. Uygulayıcıya adlandırılmış risk olarak "üç fonksiyonun gövdesi kopyalanıyor, kopya sadık mı" verdim ve kaynak migrasyona karşı doğrulamasını istedim.

Bir de push konusunu karara bağladım: planın kısıtları "push yok" diyor ama senin kararın ve `CLAUDE.md` bunun tersini söylüyor. Senin talimatın planı ezer — faz sonunda bir kez push edeceğim, görev başına değil.

I have everything needed for the verdict.

### Finding Verdict
- **The bidirectional deletes in `public.engelle` reopen a bypass of the daily 50-request cap** — ADDRESSED. `bag.istek_on_kontrol` now counts `public.istek_gunlugu` rows (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819195646_istek_gunlugu.sql:99-102`) instead of live `takipler`/`sohbet_istekleri` rows. `public.engelle` (untouched by this diff, confirmed by `git diff` stat showing only 2 files changed) never references `istek_gunlugu`, so its deletes no longer touch the counted table. The exploit path (send → engelle → counter drops → repeat) no longer works because the log row survives the delete.

### Named-risk checks
1. **Transcription fidelity** — checked line-by-line against `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql`. `bag.istek_on_kontrol` body (auth guard, self-request check, `gizli.engelli_mi` block check, `&gt;= 50` comparison) matches source exactly except the intentionally-changed `select` (source lines 13-38 vs fix lines 85-108). `public.takip_istegi_gonder` and `public.sohbet_istegi_gonder` bodies match source exactly (auth guard, `perform bag.istek_on_kontrol`, duplicate-request `exists` check, insert column list `(gonderen_id/takip_eden_id, ..., durum)` with `'beklemede'`) plus one appended `istek_gunlugu` insert each. Implementer's claimed discrepancy confirmed: source (`20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql:43`) has `grant execute on function bag.istek_on_kontrol(uuid) to authenticated;` with no matching `revoke`, and the controller's fix-1.md instruction sheet (task-9-fix-1.md:90-123) indeed omits this grant. The fix restored it verbatim (diff lines 112-116). No other discrepancy found; revoke/grant statements for the two `public.*` functions use fully-qualified `(uuid)` signatures where source omits them — functionally identical, not a defect.

2. **Does the fix close the exploit** — traced. `engelle` (unchanged, not in this diff) deletes `takipler`/`sohbet_istekleri` rows only, never `istek_gunlugu`. `engeli_kaldir`, `takip_istegini_yanitla`, `sohbet_istegini_yanitla`, `takibi_birak`, `takipciyi_cikar` are all untouched by this diff and none references `istek_gunlugu`. The log table has RLS enabled with zero policies (controller-verified live; source confirms `alter table public.istek_gunlugu enable row level security;` with no `create policy` anywhere in the file), so no client-side path can delete or decrement it; only the two send RPCs (security definer, bypass RLS as owner) insert into it, and only ever insert, never delete/update. Conclusion: exploit is closed, and no new deletion path against the log was introduced.

3. **Log-write placement** — confirmed. In both `public.takip_istegi_gonder` and `public.sohbet_istegi_gonder`, the `istek_gunlugu` insert is the last statement in the function body, after the successful `takipler`/`sohbet_istekleri` insert and after the duplicate-request `raise` check. Since there is no exception-handling block, any `raise` (auth guard, `bag.istek_on_kontrol`'s internal raises, or the duplicate-request check) aborts the whole function invocation atomically before the log insert is reached, and nothing after the log insert can fail — so a failed send leaves no log row, and a successful send writes exactly one.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
None.

### Out-of-Scope Observations
- `bag.istek_on_kontrol`'s ACL still lacks `revoke execute ... from public, anon` (PUBLIC still holds EXECUTE per controller's live-DB check) — this predates the fix (Task 7) and the fix's re-creation of the function preserves the same source-faithful grant-only pattern rather than worsening it. Already ledgered by the controller; not reintroduced or worsened here.

### Verdict
**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage. Test claims verified: `sema-dogrula.ts` diff shows exactly two new `esitMi` assertions matching the report's described observed behavior (empty-array select, `42501` insert rejection), consistent with 38→40 OK-line count reported.</result>

<result>### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant. All Task 10 deliverables are present and match the brief exactly: `mobil/lib/bag.ts` (79 lines) and `mobil/lib/bag.test.ts` (71 lines), no other files touched.
- Missing: none. Extra: none. Misunderstood: none.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff alone: whether `bagDurumunuGetir`'s query shape (table/column names) is correct in the *live* database — verified instead by reading the migration files directly (see Named-risk checks below), since this diff contains no schema.

### Named-risk checks

**Risk 1 — exported contract matches brief exactly.**
Checked `mobil/lib/bag.ts` line-by-line against the brief's "Produces" list:
- `BagDurumu = 'yok' | 'beklemede' | 'kabul'` — bag.ts:3, exact match.
- `BagKisi = { id, kullaniciAdi, ad }` — bag.ts:5-9, exact match (unused within this file, correctly exported for Tasks 14/15 to consume).
- All seven functions present with exact names, parameter lists, and return types from the brief: `takipIstegiGonder`, `takipIsteginiYanitla`, `takibiBirak`, `takipciyiCikar`, `sohbetIstegiGonder`, `sohbetIsteginiYanitla`, `bagDurumunuGetir(kullaniciId): Promise&lt;{ takip: BagDurumu; sohbet: BagDurumu }&gt;` — bag.ts:117-190. No renames, no shape drift. **Contract is safe for Tasks 14/15.**
- Cross-checked all six RPC name strings and their `p_kullanici_id`/`p_kabul` parameter names against `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql:45-210` (function signatures `takip_istegi_gonder(p_kullanici_id uuid)`, `takip_istegini_yanitla(p_kullanici_id uuid, p_kabul boolean)`, etc.) — all match verbatim.

**Risk 2 — relationship direction and state distinction in `bagDurumunuGetir`.**
Read `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819183239_takipler.sql:1-13` and `20260819183711_sohbet_istekleri.sql:1-13`. Design comment confirms: "Takip tek yonlu: A, B'yi takip etmek icin istek gonderir" — the relevant relationship for "which button to show on someone else's profile" is *my* outgoing request/follow toward them. `bagDurumunuGetir` (bag.ts:170-175, 178-183) queries `takip_eden_id = benimId` / `takip_edilen_id = kullaniciId` and `gonderen_id = benimId` / `alan_id = kullaniciId` — this is the correct direction (viewer as the initiating side), matching the function's own doc comment (bag.ts:160-164). `durum` is DB-constrained to exactly `'beklemede'`/`'kabul'` (`takipler_durum` check, migration line 13), and `'yok'` is correctly synthesized from `maybeSingle()` returning `null` (bag.ts:187-188). No direction mix-up found.

**Risk 3 — error-surfacing convention.**
Checked `mobil/lib/kisi-ara.ts:26` and `mobil/lib/profil.ts:25` — both do plain `throw new Error(error.message)`, same as bag.ts's `rpcCagir` (bag.ts:131-134) and the two `bagDurumunuGetir` select error checks (bag.ts:176, 184). This is consistent with the codebase's actual convention: the RPCs themselves raise Turkish-language exceptions by design (`'Bugunluk istek sinirina ulastin'`, `'Istegin zaten gonderilmis'`, `'Yanitlanacak istek bulunamadi'`, confirmed in `20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql`), so the client layer's job is just to forward `error.message`, not translate it. The one place I found genuine code-based translation is `mobil/lib/kullanici-adi.ts:36-42`, which special-cases Postgres error codes (`23505`, `23514`) specifically because `kullanici_adi_degistir` can hit a raw unique-constraint race. `bag.ts` follows the majority-pattern module, correctly.

### Strengths
- Contract fidelity is exact — zero drift from the brief's types and signatures, which is the thing Tasks 14/15 most depend on.
- `bagDurumunuGetir`'s relationship-direction handling was independently cross-checked against the schema and is correct, including the subtle "who initiated" semantics.
- Error handling matches the codebase's dominant convention rather than reinventing one.
- Report includes real verbatim TDD evidence (RED then GREEN) and a genuine byte-level BOM check.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **Test coverage has real gaps for a shared-contract module — plan-mandated.** `mobil/lib/bag.test.ts` (verbatim copy of the brief's Step 1) tests only 4 of the 7 exported functions. `takipciyiCikar`, `sohbetIstegiGonder`, and `sohbetIsteginiYanitla` have **zero** test coverage — not even a "called with correct params" assertion. Additionally: `takipIsteginiYanitla` is only tested with `kabul: false` (never `true`), `bagDurumunuGetir`'s error branches (`takipHatasi`/`sohbetHatasi`) and the `'beklemede'` state are never exercised, and `kendiKullaniciId`'s `'Oturum bulunamadi'` throw path (bag.ts:127) has no test. The single `bagDurumunuGetir` test's mock chain (`bag.test.ts:76-78`) also doesn't assert which table/columns are queried, so it wouldn't catch a column-name typo — that risk was only closed by the implementer's manual cross-check against migrations, not by the test suite. Since this module is the exact contract Tasks 14 and 15 build screens on, an undetected bug in any of the three untested functions (e.g., a wrong RPC-name string) would silently surface as a broken button two tasks later — precisely the failure mode this review round is meant to catch. This gap originates in the brief's own Step 1 test file, so it is plan-mandated, not an implementer shortcut; still, given the functions are structurally trivial one-line RPC wrappers identical in shape to their tested siblings, the actual bug risk is low. Recommend adding at minimum one "called with correct params" test per untested function and one error-path/`'beklemede'` test for `bagDurumunuGetir` before Task 14/15 begin relying on this module.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **`bag.ts:151-165` — sequential network calls in `bagDurumunuGetir`.** The two `.from(...).select(...)` queries run one after another rather than via `Promise.all`, adding avoidable latency to a profile-screen load. Low impact, easy follow-up.
2. **`bag.ts:58-67` (RPC) — theoretical raw-Postgres-error leak on rapid double-submit.** `takip_istegi_gonder`/`sohbet_istegi_gonder` do an `exists` pre-check before insert with no atomic guard (no `ON CONFLICT`), so a fast double-tap could in principle hit the primary-key constraint and surface a raw `"duplicate key value violates ..."` message through `rpcCagir`'s plain pass-through (bag.ts:131-134), unlike `kullanici-adi.ts`'s explicit `23505` translation for its similar race. Narrow edge case (requires a genuine double-submit race), and not something Task 10's client wrapper is positioned to fix — the atomicity gap is server-side. Worth a note for whoever owns Task 7's RPCs, not a blocker here.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The exported contract, relationship-direction logic, and error-surfacing convention — the three things this review was asked to verify most carefully — are all correct and will not break Tasks 14/15. The one Important finding is a coverage gap inherited verbatim from the brief's own test file (3 of 7 functions with zero coverage), which is real but low-risk given the untested functions are trivial and structurally identical to already-tested siblings; it should be closed before those functions get materially more complex, but does not block this task's approval.</result>

<result>### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant

All three required behaviors from the brief's Step 3 table are implemented exactly as specified, with exact button text matches: `mobil/src/app/kullanici/[id].tsx:237-267`. The four brief-mandated tests are present verbatim in `mobil/__tests__/ekranlar/kullanici/[id].test.tsx:87-120`. Consumed functions match the Task 10 contract exactly (`lib/bag.ts` verified: `bagDurumunuGetir` scopes by `takip_eden_id = benimId` / `gonderen_id = benimId`, confirming viewer-as-initiator semantics as documented). No extra scope added (`takipIsteginiYanitla`, `takipciyiCikar`, `sohbetIsteginiYanitla` correctly left unused on this screen, only mocked because the module exports them). No misunderstandings found.

### Named-risk checks

1. **Relationship direction** — Checked. Traced all three `BagDurumu` values for both axes (`[id].tsx:237-267`): `yok`→"Takip et"/"Sohbet iste" (actionable), `beklemede`→"Istek gonderildi" (non-pressable `View`), `kabul`→"Takibi birak" (actionable) / "Sohbet acik" (non-pressable). Since `bagDurumunuGetir` only reports rows where the viewer is the initiator, an incoming (not-yet-sent-by-viewer) request correctly falls through to `yok`→"Takip et", never surfacing as a false "accept" affordance. No misrepresentation.

2. **State refresh after action** — Checked. All three handlers (`takipEt`, `takibiBirakEt`, `sohbetIste`, `[id].tsx:185-213`) optimistically set exact post-success state only inside the `try` block after `await` resolves; on rejection the `catch` sets `hata` and leaves `bagDurum` untouched, so the UI never claims an action succeeded when it didn't. Consistent with the pre-existing `engelle` handler's pattern in the same file.

3. **Silent blocking preserved** — Checked. All three new catch blocks use the unmodified pattern `e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Bir sorun olustu'` (`[id].tsx:190-191, 200-201, 210-211`), identical to the pre-existing error handling already in this file. `'Bu kullanici bulunamadi'` would render verbatim, undecorated. No other diff content leaks block state.

4. **Async testing-library usage** — Checked line by line. Every `render()` call is awaited (lines 89, 98, 107, 117) and every `fireEvent.press()` is awaited (lines 99, 118); `findByText` calls (async) are awaited throughout; synchronous `getByText`/`queryByText` calls correctly omit `await`. No violations.

Also independently verified: no BOM, no non-ASCII bytes in either changed file (`grep -nP '[^\x00-\x7F]'` returned nothing; first bytes are `696d 706f...` = "impo...", no BOM prefix). Test file correctly lives under `mobil/__tests__/ekranlar/kullanici/`, not under `mobil/src/app`.

### Strengths
- Button-text-to-state mapping is a direct, unembellished transcription of the brief's table — no invented copy, no scope creep.
- Optimistic local-state updates only fire after the awaited call resolves, so success/failure semantics are correctly reflected without extra network round-trips.
- Error handling, styling conventions, and handler naming (`takipEt`, `sikayetEt`, etc.) are consistent with the rest of the file's pre-existing style.
- TDD evidence in the report is credible: stash/pop cycle showing the 4 tests genuinely fail-then-pass, plus a full-suite run with no regressions (185→189 green).

### Issues

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **Two of three new write handlers have zero test coverage.** `takibiBirakEt` (`[id].tsx:195-203`, wired to the "Takibi birak" button at line 248) and `sohbetIste` (`[id].tsx:205-213`, wired to "Sohbet iste" at line 254) are never exercised by a `fireEvent.press` anywhere in the diff — only their *rendering* is indirectly checked (test at line 105-109 confirms "Takibi birak" appears, but never presses it; "Sohbet iste" is only checked for presence in the first test, never pressed). Consequently: the `sohbet==='beklemede'` and `sohbet==='kabul'` ("Sohbet acik") render branches (lines 258-267) are also completely untested, and there is no error-path test for either handler. Since both handlers call live RPCs (`takibiBirak`, `sohbetIstegiGonder`) that mutate real relationship state, a wiring bug (wrong id, wrong state transition, mis-typed button text) in either would ship undetected. This mirrors the task instructions' own framing ("four tests for seven interactive paths") — it is a real coverage gap, not a nitpick, even though the code is a structural copy of the one handler (`takipEt`) that *is* tested. Note this traces directly to the brief's Step 1 test list, which specifies only these four tests verbatim — the implementer followed the brief exactly rather than cutting a corner, so this is plan-mandated under-coverage, not an implementer shortcut. Recommend adding presses for "Takibi birak" and "Sohbet iste" (success + at least one failure case) before trusting this screen's write paths.

### Minor (Nice to Have)
- **Duplicated "Istek gonderildi" block.** The identical `&lt;View style={stiller.pasifButon}&gt;&lt;Text style={stiller.pasifButonYazi}&gt;Istek gonderildi&lt;/Text&gt;&lt;/View&gt;` JSX appears twice (`[id].tsx:242-246` and `258-262`), once per axis. A small local helper (e.g. `&lt;PasifDurum metin="Istek gonderildi" /&gt;`) would remove the duplication, but the current form is simple enough that this is cosmetic only.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** All four named risks check out clean, spec compliance is exact including literal Turkish button text, and the silent-blocking/error-passthrough behavior is correctly preserved. The one Important finding (missing test coverage for `takibiBirakEt`/`sohbetIste`) is a real gap in verification, but the affected code is a straightforward structural mirror of the one handler that is tested, was written per the brief's own test list, and manual inspection found no defect in it — so it's a should-fix-before-full-trust item rather than a blocking correctness failure.</result>

<result>Confirmed. I now have everything needed for the review.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant — the four required files exist at the correct paths, the migration follows the security-definer checklist exactly, the module produces the three contracted functions plus the accepted `gidenIstekleriGetir` addition, and the screen implements all four sections with the specified per-row actions.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether `bag_kisileri` was actually applied against the live remote database (report claims `supabase db push` succeeded and shows a manual ad-hoc verification script's output, but that script isn't in the diff and the push log isn't independently checkable here).

### Named-risk checks

1. **Join trap.** Checked every query in `mobil/lib/bag-listeleri.ts`. `kimlikleriOku` (bag-listeleri.ts:24–32) issues `.from(tablo).select(sutun).eq(...).eq(...)` against `takipler`/`sohbet_istekleri` only, selecting a single scalar column, never `profiller` and never a nested/embedded select syntax. Name resolution goes exclusively through `kisileriCoz` → `supabase.rpc('bag_kisileri', ...)` (bag-listeleri.ts:8–20). All four exported functions (`gelenIstekleriGetir`, `gidenIstekleriGetir`, `takipcilerimiGetir`, `takipEttiklerimiGetir`) route through this same pair. No embedded join against `profiller` anywhere in the file. Risk cleared.

2. **Recipient-only semantics.** Verified against the live RPC source in `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql`: `takip_istegini_yanitla` only matches rows where `takip_edilen_id = auth.uid()` (lines 91–92, 97–98), i.e., caller must be the recipient. In `baglar.tsx`, `takipIstegineYanitVer`/`sohbetIstegineYanitVer` (accept/reject buttons) are wired only into the "Gelen istekler" and "Sohbet istekleri" FlatLists, whose data comes from `gelenIstekleriGetir()` — built by `kimlikleriOku('takipler', 'takip_eden_id', 'takip_edilen_id', benimId, 'beklemede')`, i.e., condition column `takip_edilen_id = benimId` (I am recipient), returned column `takip_eden_id` (the sender's id, correctly passed as `p_kullanici_id`). The "Giden istekler" section (built from the mirrored column selection in `gidenIstekleriGetir`) renders only a static "Istek gonderildi" label with no buttons (baglar.tsx:574–588). Follower/following direction checked too: `takipcilerimiGetir` conditions on `takip_edilen_id = benimId` (correct — followers are people who follow me), `takipEttiklerimiGetir` conditions on `takip_eden_id = benimId` (correct — people I follow). All four directions are correct; no accept-button exposure on outgoing requests.

3. **Async testing-library usage.** Grepped every `render(` and `fireEvent.` call in `mobil/__tests__/ekranlar/baglar.test.tsx` — all 14 occurrences are `await`ed (lines 57–58, 71, 85–86, 97–98, 110–111, 123–124, 135, 146–147, 157). Clean.

4. **Test coverage across four sections.** Coverage is real but partial, not a slice. Covered: takip-request accept (bag-listeleri.ts consumer), takip-request reject, sohbet-request accept, engelle from the incoming list, followers list + remove (`Cikar`), following list + unfollow (`Takibi birak`), outgoing list rendering. Not covered: **sohbet-request reject** (no test presses "Reddet" on a chat request), **engelle from the followers section** (only exercised from the incoming-requests section, though it's the same `kullaniciyiEngelle` handler), any of the five `ListEmptyComponent` empty-state strings, the top-level error path (`hata` state after a failed action or failed initial load), and the loading state. This is broader coverage than a bare slice but leaves real gaps, particularly the untested reject-path for chat requests and the entirely untested error/empty states — rated Minor per calibration, but worth listing explicitly since the brief's Step 4 sample only tests 3 of 9+ interactive behaviors and the implementer added 6 more without reaching full parity.

### Strengths
- The anti-join lesson from Faz 2a (decision #18) is applied correctly and consistently — every list-fetching path resolves names via the `bag_kisileri` RPC, never a join, with a clear comment explaining why (bag-listeleri.ts:9–13).
- Direction handling for four different follow/request relationships is correct in every case, verified against the actual RPC source rather than assumed.
- The `bag_kisileri` migration matches the brief's guarded-RPC checklist exactly: `security definer`, `set search_path = public`, `auth.uid() is null` guard first, `revoke ... from public, anon` + `grant ... to authenticated`, and filters blocked users via `gizli.engelli_mi`.
- `kullaniciyiEngelle` (baglar.tsx:494–507) removes the blocked user from all six local state arrays at once, so a block doesn't leave a stale row in another section of the same screen — this wasn't explicitly demanded by the brief but is exactly the right defensive behavior.
- The `as unknown as` cast claim in the report checks out — it matches the established idiom already used three times in `mobil/lib/checkin.ts:86,96,111`, not a novel workaround.
- All five new/changed files are byte-verified ASCII-clean and BOM-free; no already-applied migration was touched (only one new migration file added).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `mobil/__tests__/ekranlar/baglar.test.tsx`: no test exercises rejecting a chat request (only takip-reject is tested), and no test covers the `hata` error-display path or any `ListEmptyComponent` string. Given this screen has five distinct empty states and a shared error banner, at least one error-path test (e.g., a rejected `takipIsteginiYanitla` promise still surfacing `hata`) would meaningfully raise confidence.
- `mobil/src/app/baglar.tsx:517–630`: five FlatLists repeat near-identical row JSX (kisiBilgisi block + button row) with only the button set differing. Not large enough to be a hard problem at 258 lines, but a shared `BagSatiri` row component would remove real duplication if this screen grows further (e.g., pagination, avatars).
- `mobil/lib/bag-listeleri.ts:24–32`: `kimlikleriOku`'s parameter list (`tablo, sutun, kosulSutunu, kosulDegeri, durum`) is easy to transpose by mistake since three of the five parameters are same-typed strings with similar names; a positional call error (e.g., swapping `sutun`/`kosulSutunu`) would compile silently. Low risk here since all four call sites were verified by hand, but a call-site with named arguments or an options object would be safer if this helper gains more callers.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** All four named risks check out under direct inspection of the actual diff and the live RPC/schema sources it depends on — no join trap, correct recipient-only wiring in both the RPC layer and the screen, fully async test calls, and reasonably (if not exhaustively) broad test coverage. The only findings are minor coverage gaps and a code-quality nit, neither of which threatens correctness.</result>

- Turkish user-facing strings ("Baglar") correct; code/comments/test names are plain ASCII, confirmed by direct byte scan (see below) — no unverifiable claims here.

### Named-risk checks

1. **Counting rule.** Read `mobil/lib/bag-listeleri.ts:48-62`. `gelenIstekleriGetir` queries `takipler` with `kosulSutunu='takip_edilen_id'` set to my id (i.e. rows where *I* am the target — someone else initiated), and `sohbet_istekleri` with `alan_id=benimId` (I am the receiver) — both correctly incoming. Contrast with `gidenIstekleriGetir` (line 69-83) which reverses these columns. `index.tsx:120-122` sums `istekler.takip.length + istekler.sohbet.length` from the incoming call only; `gidenIstekleriGetir` is never imported or called in the diff. **No defect** — counting rule is correctly implemented.

2. **Zero-count case.** `index.tsx:140`: `{bekleyenSayisi &gt; 0 &amp;&amp; &lt;Text ...&gt;}` — badge `Text` node is not rendered at all when count is 0, not rendered as "0". Test `index.test.tsx:63-69` asserts `queryByText(/^\d+$/)` is null after the fetch resolves with empty lists, and confirmed no other digit-text exists elsewhere on this screen (checked full render output, `index.tsx:20-48`). **No defect.**

3. **Load cost / failure isolation.** `index.tsx:119-123`: the `useEffect` fires `gelenIstekleriGetir()` with `.then(...).catch(() =&gt; setBekleyenSayisi(0))` — the catch swallows any error (network, missing session) and falls back to 0 without throwing, and the component's JSX render is synchronous and unconditional (doesn't await the fetch). Test `index.test.tsx:85-93` exercises the reject path and confirms "Baglar" text still renders with no badge. **No defect** — a failed/slow request cannot blank the screen.

4. **Async testing-library usage.** Every `render(&lt;AnaEkran /&gt;)` and `fireEvent.press(...)` call in `index.test.tsx` (lines 26-27, 34-35, 40-41, 46-47, 52-53, 58-59, 64, 79, 87) is preceded by `await`. **No defect.**

### Strengths
- Badge markup keeps "Baglar" as its own isolated `Text` node (`index.tsx:139`) specifically so `getByText('Baglar')` exact-match continues to work with the badge present — a real, verified design constraint, not just an assertion in the report.
- Error handling degrades gracefully (count silently falls back to 0) without surfacing a distracting error on a screen whose primary job is navigation, consistent with this being a secondary/decorative affordance.

None.

The third account's username is derived from its own UUID (`kullanici_${c.id.slice(0, 8)}`, yardimcilar.ts:66) — that is derivation from data, not a fixed pattern, and it fits `^[a-z0-9._]{3,20}$` at 18 chars with lowercase hex. I checked the new C profile for the exact regression class the constraint names: scenarios 14/15 (calistir.ts:413-466) and sema-dogrula.ts's wildcard probes (sema-dogrula.ts:114-140, which already derives the wildcard position from B's real name) all use `.some(s =&gt; s.id === bId)` rather than exact-set or count equality, so a third permanent profile named "Ucuncu Test Kullanici" cannot perturb them. `dogum_tarihi: '2000-01-01'` is fixed but permanently satisfies an 18+ constraint.

### Strengths

- The repair is honestly mechanical. Reading the removed lines rather than the report, the fix really is one parameter name plus a return-shape unwrap, and it genuinely restores 11 assertions without touching a single expectation. That is the outcome the brief asked for and the hardest one to fake.
- Scenario 23 asserts the *reversal* of Faz 2b decision #25 head-on (calistir.ts:706, 712) — this is the assertion most likely to catch a regression to the old rule, and it was written strictly.
- Positive controls in 22/24/28 (calistir.ts:690, 729, 846) are the right pattern: each proves visibility exists immediately before the step that should remove it, so the negative cannot pass by accident.
- Scenario 27 tests `bag.ani_gorunurlugu` through the real `check_inden_ayril` path rather than calling the internal helper, and asserts the exact string in both directions — including the "does not widen" case, which is the direction a naive implementation gets wrong.
- The `--tavan` gate (calistir.ts:16, 891-895) prints an explicit ATLANDI line instead of silently omitting the scenario, so a reader of the default output cannot mistake 29 for passing.
- README documents *why* the cap scenario is destructive and irreversible within a calendar day (README.md:150-171) — an operator reading only the README would not accidentally poison their own run.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)

None.

#### Important (Should Fix)

**1. Scenario 23's "C cannot see" has no proof that C is at the venue — its precondition is inherited silently from scenario 22.** `mobil/gorunurluk-testleri/calistir.ts:701-716`. C's presence at mekan-1 comes solely from the check-in created at calistir.ts:686 inside scenario 22; scenario 23 creates nothing for C and asserts nothing about C's state. This matters because the entire point of the assertion at calistir.ts:712 is that same-venue presence no longer overrides `gizli` — if C happened not to be live at mekan-1 (someone reorders scenarios, deletes 22, or a future scenario closes C's check-in), the assertion passes with a broken implementation and the karar #39 regression ships. Related: nowhere in 22 or 23 is C's client shown to be able to *see* anything, so a C-side read that returns empty for an unrelated reason reads as a pass. Fix: add one line in scenario 22 after C's check-in — `esitMi(await aniGorulebiliyorMu(c, cCi), true, 'saglama: C kendi canli check-in-ini gorur')` — and re-establish or re-assert C's presence at the top of scenario 23 (a single `esitMi(await aniGorulebiliyorMu(c, cCi), true, ...)` using a `cCi` hoisted out of 22, or a fresh `checkInYap(c, ...)` inside 23). Both are cheap and make the scenario self-contained.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

**2. Scenarios 20 and 21 discard the result of `takip_istegi_gonder`.** `calistir.ts:640` and `calistir.ts:654`. The RPC's error is not destructured, so a failing send is invisible. In scenario 20 this is the whole setup: with no pending request, the assertion at 643-647 still passes, just for a weaker reason than the scenario name claims. Scenario 21 is protected by its `kabulHatasi === null` assertion at 671, and scenario 19 immediately before proves the send path works, so the suite as a whole is covered — but the individual scenario is not. Note this code was supplied verbatim in the brief, so the implementer followed instructions. Fix: destructure and `esitMi(gonderHata, null, ...)`, or accept the pre-existing pending request from 20 explicitly rather than re-sending in 21.

**3. Deferred cleanup for `takipler` and `engellemeler` is silent, contradicting the brief's "sessiz birakilmaz".** `yardimcilar.ts:149-154` (new) and `yardimcilar.ts:142-147` (pre-existing). Entries pushed at calistir.ts:641 and 655 (follows) and calistir.ts:736 (block) are cleaned only via `console.error` in `temizle()`, not via `esitMi`. The brief demands `esitMi`-asserted cleanup but its own supplied `temizle()` snippet uses `console.error`, so this is an inconsistency in the brief rather than a deviation from it — the scenarios that clean up inline (19 at calistir.ts:631, 26 at 780, 28 at 851) do assert with `esitMi`. Fix: count cleanup failures as assertions in `temizle()`, applying the same treatment the storage-delete already got at calistir.ts:571-576.

**4. Scenario 30 asserts only that an error occurred, not which.** `calistir.ts:908, 910`. A renamed RPC or a typo'd parameter would also error and pass. I checked the signature and grant for `bag_kisileri` and both are correct today, and this mirrors the pre-existing scenario 17 pattern, so the risk is latent rather than present. Fix: assert on the error code (`42501`/`PGRST301`) rather than mere non-nullness.

**5. Scenario 24 cannot distinguish "row deleted" from "row hidden by RLS".** `calistir.ts:737-745`. A queries `takipler` after blocking B; both parties are blocked, so an RLS-hidden-but-present row is indistinguishable from a deleted one. The paired visibility assertion at 748 covers the behavioral consequence, and scenario 28 checks the same table with no block in effect (calistir.ts:853-859), so the deletion is proven elsewhere. No client-side fix exists without a service-role key; worth a comment noting scenario 28 carries that proof.

**6. The `TAVAN_MODU` comment misstates what the flag does.** `calistir.ts:12` says "`--tavan`: yalnizca senaryo 29'u ... calistirir" — the flag *adds* scenario 29 to the full run; all 30 scenarios execute. README.md:161-166 describes it correctly. Fix the comment to match.

**7. Two new em dashes (U+2014) in code comments.** `calistir.ts:591` and `calistir.ts:826`. The constraint says code and comments are plain ASCII. No Turkish accented characters and no U+2019 were introduced (I enumerated every non-ASCII codepoint across all added lines: U+2014 only), and the file already contained em dashes at lines 203 and 345 plus a pre-existing `ör.` at line 87, so this is consistency drift, not new breakage. Both files are UTF-8 without BOM (verified: first bytes `69 6d 70` / `69 6d 70` / `23 20 47`).

**8. The pre-scenario-19 unblock sits outside a `senaryo()` wrapper.** `calistir.ts:596-599`. If `engeli_kaldir` fails, the thrown error escapes `main()` and `temizle(t)` at calistir.ts:914 never runs, leaving live check-in rows behind. This exactly mirrors the pre-existing pre-scenario-11 block at calistir.ts:347-350, so it is house style rather than a new defect.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The repair is genuine — I accounted for all 22 removed code lines and not one assertion, expected value, or message was touched, and scenarios 7 and 8 are provably setup-free bodies whose failures could only have been cascades from scenario 5's throw. The 12 new scenarios match their table rows and mostly carry real positive controls; the one gap worth fixing before this is relied on as the phase's proof is scenario 23's unasserted C-at-the-venue precondition (Important #1), a two-line change.</result>

Şimdi **fazın tamamının geniş incelemesi** çalışıyor — 27 commit, 200 KB'lık diff, en yetenekli modelle. Bu incelemenin değeri tek tek görev incelemelerinin **yapısal olarak göremediği** şeyde: her biri yalnızca kendi diff'ini gördü. Ona üç şey sordum:

**Görünürlük modelinin uçtan uca izi** — bir check-in'in oluşumundan, canlı görünürlüğünden, anıya dönüşümünden, kimin okuyabildiğine kadar; RLS politikası, üç dönüşüm yolu, istemci modülleri ve ekranlar boyunca. Görünürlüğün kullanıcının seçtiğinden **daha geniş** kaldığı herhangi bir giriş, sıralama ya da durum kombinasyonu var mı. Bu fazın çekirdek vaadi bu — ve zaten bu turda tam bu sınıftan iki gerçek kusur bulmuştuk.

**Engellemenin bütünlüğü** — sessiz kalması ve her yüzeyde geçerli olması.

**Görevlerin dikiş yerleri** — 18 görev farklı ajanlar tarafından yazıldı; ayrışmış kurallar ve birbirini tutmayan varsayımlar.

Ayrıca ertelediğim 17 minor bulguyu triyaj etmesini istedim: hangisi birleştirmeden önce düzeltilmeli, hangisi böyle gidebilir. Kimsenin bakmadığı bir erteleme listesi, sessizce çöpe atılmış demektir.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>### Summary

The SQL half of this branch is careful, coherent work: the three-tier model matches the spec table line for line, the "visibility only ever narrows" rule is factored into one helper (`bag.ani_gorunurlugu`) and genuinely called from all three conversion paths, the two new relationship tables are RPC-only by construction, and blocking severs follows in both directions. I found no defect *inside* the diff that widens visibility. What I did find is that the phase's central gate now leans on a pre-existing hole nobody closed: `authenticated` holds unrestricted column-level UPDATE on `check_inler`, so `mekan_id` — the input to `gizli.ayni_mekanda_canli_mi()`, which the new policy uses to decide who may read a live check-in and its exact GPS point — is client-forgeable. That is a two-line migration, and Faz 2c already set the precedent for it on `profiller`. With that fixed plus the fail-open default and the bulk memory-widening setter, I'd merge. Without them, the phase's promise does not hold in production.

### How I read the diff

Four passes, in this order. (1) All 12 migrations end to end — this is where the security lives, and I read them as a sequence because three of them rewrite functions the earlier ones created. (2) I then left the diff to read the *pre-existing* migrations the new policy depends on (`gizli.engelli_mi`, `gizli.ayni_mekanda_canli_mi`, the `check_inler` update policy, `yakin_mekanlar_yogunluk`, `baskasinin_profili`, `kisi_ara`) — a task-scoped review cannot see these, and the new policy is meaningless without them. (3) The four client modules (`bag.ts`, `bag-listeleri.ts`, `checkin.ts`, `ayarlar.ts`), tracing every write path that can touch `bulunurluk` or `gorunurluk`. (4) The five screens plus the live-test scenario list, to see which claims are actually proven against a real database and which are only mocked.

### Findings

#### Critical (blocks merge)

**1. `check_inler` accepts arbitrary column updates from the client, which forges the venue gate the new policy depends on**

`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260816180000_check_inler_recursion_ve_guncelleme.sql:53-56` is the only UPDATE policy on the table:

```sql
create policy "kendi check-in'ini guncelleyebilir"
  on public.check_inler for update to authenticated
  using (kullanici_id = auth.uid()) with check (kullanici_id = auth.uid());
```

RLS is row-level, not column-level, and no `revoke update … grant update (col)` was ever applied to `check_inler` (grepped all 51 migrations — the only grants are on `profiller`). The blanket privilege is confirmed empirically: `mobil/gorunurluk-testleri/calistir.ts:166-167` performs a direct client `.from('check_inler').update({ gorunurluk: 'kimse' })` and passes against the live database.

So an authenticated user can PATCH their own check-in row's `mekan_id`, `bitis_zamani`, `konum` and `kullanici_adi`. The consequence lands squarely on this phase: `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819185621_check_inler_bag_gorunurlugu.sql:25-28` grants read of a `herkese_acik` live check-in to anyone for whom `gizli.ayni_mekanda_canli_mi(check_inler.mekan_id)` is true — and that helper just asks whether the *caller* has a live row with that `mekan_id`. Do one real check-in anywhere, then repoint your own row's `mekan_id` at any venue in the country and push `bitis_zamani` out a year: you now read every `herkese_acik` live check-in at that venue, permanently, without going there. And `mobil/lib/checkin.ts:82` selects `konum`, which `check_in_yap` fills with the *device's* coordinates, not the venue's — so what leaks is the target's GPS point, not "was at venue X".

Forging `kullanici_adi` on your own row additionally spoofs your display name in the "su an burda" list.

This predates the branch, but Faz 3a is the phase that makes it exploitable at range, and Faz 2c already wrote the fix and the reasoning for it in `20260819123253_profiller_sutun_yetkileri.sql:1-10` — *"Istemcinin cagirmayi secebilecegi bir kural, kural degildir."* Apply the same treatment:

```sql
revoke update on public.check_inler from authenticated;
grant update (gorunurluk) on public.check_inler to authenticated;
```

`gorunurluk` is the only column any client code writes directly (`lib/ayarlar.ts:82`); everything else already goes through `security definer` RPCs, so nothing else breaks.

#### Important (should fix before merge)

**2. One tap in settings republishes every memory the user hid — including ones from `gizli` check-ins**

`mobil/lib/ayarlar.ts:76-85` writes `gorunurluk` to *every* row of the user unconditionally:

```ts
.from('check_inler').update({ gorunurluk: deger }).eq('kullanici_id', id)
```

`bag.ani_gorunurlugu` guarantees a `gizli` check-in becomes a `kimse` memory. This setter, reached from `src/app/profil/ayarlar.tsx:167-169` ("Herkes gorsun"), overwrites that to `herkese_acik` — and a `herkese_acik` memory is readable by every authenticated user in the system. Every past check-in the user deliberately marked hidden becomes public in one tap, with no confirmation and no display of the current value. The row still carries its original `bulunurluk`, so the intent is recoverable, but no code recovers it.

The branch states the invariant "gorunurluk HICBIR ZAMAN genislemez" (`20260819194021_ani_gorunurlugu_yardimcisi.sql:547`) and then leaves the one client path that violates it unguarded. Fix server-side so the client cannot exceed it: a `security definer` RPC that sets `gorunurluk = bag.ani_gorunurlugu(bulunurluk, p_deger)` per row, which clamps each memory to its own original choice. (This also falls out of finding 1's column revoke — after it, the direct update stops working and an RPC is required anyway.)

**3. The check-in screen fails open on the user's saved default visibility**

`src/app/check-in/[mekanId].tsx:28` initialises `useState&lt;Bulunurluk&gt;('herkese_acik')` — the widest tier — and only later overwrites it from the profile at lines 36-41, with `.catch(() =&gt; {})` swallowing failures. The submit button is enabled the entire time. A user whose stored default is `gizli` who taps quickly, or whose profile read fails on a bad connection, broadcasts `herkese_acik` instead. The shape predates the branch, but the cost is higher now: `herkese_acik` no longer means "people at this venue" — it means the venue *plus every follower, anywhere*.

Fix: keep the submit button disabled until the default resolves, and on failure fall back to `gizli` (or surface the error) rather than to the widest value.

**4. `baglar.tsx` stacks five VirtualizedLists inside a non-scrolling `View`**

`src/app/baglar.tsx:122, 148, 174, 190, 213` are five `FlatList`s and six headers inside `kapsayici: { flex: 1 }` (line 234) with no `ScrollView` and no `SectionList`. The container cannot scroll, so on a phone the later sections — "Takipcilerim" and "Takip ettiklerim", the two that carry the "Cikar" and "Takibi birak" controls — are very likely clipped and unreachable, and each list scrolls independently inside its own slice. `kisiler.tsx` uses one list; `mekanlar/[id].tsx` stacks two; five is a new scale and a divergence from both.

I could not confirm the clipping without rendering — the screen tests (`__tests__/ekranlar/baglar.test.tsx`) mount with `@testing-library/react-native`, which never lays out, so the suite is structurally blind to this. Fix: a single `SectionList`, or a `ScrollView` wrapping `FlatList`s with `scrollEnabled={false}`.

**5. A sent chat request can never be withdrawn — by anyone, at any layer**

`sohbet_istegini_yanitla` (`20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql:388-418`) only matches `alan_id = auth.uid()`, `sohbet_istekleri` has no INSERT/DELETE policy, and no other RPC touches it. So once B sends A a chat request, B cannot retract it: it sits in A's inbox until A acts or one of them blocks the other, and `sohbet_istegi_gonder` will raise "Istegin zaten gonderilmis" forever. In an app whose stated core risk is unwanted contact with strangers, "I mis-sent this, take it back" is not a nicety.

Follow requests are better off — `takibi_birak` (`:322-336`) deletes regardless of `durum`, so the primitive exists — but no screen calls it for a pending request either: `src/app/baglar.tsx:174-188` renders outgoing requests as inert text, and `src/app/kullanici/[id].tsx:242-246` and `:258-262` render "Istek gonderildi" as a `View`, not a `Pressable`. Fix: add a `sohbet_istegini_geri_cek` RPC, and make both pending states pressable.

**6. The selected-state styling makes the privacy control the hardest thing to read**

`src/app/profil/ayarlar.tsx:202-203`: `butonSecili` sets `backgroundColor: '#111'` while `butonMetni` keeps `color: '#0645ad'`. Blue on near-black is roughly 2.2:1 — well under the 4.5:1 floor — and that chip is the *only* indicator of the user's current default check-in visibility. Give the selected chip white text.

Separately, the "Butun anilarimi kim gorsun" trio at `:163-183` has no selected state at all and never shows the current value, so the control in finding 2 gives no feedback either before or after the tap.

#### Minor (can ship)

**7.** `20260819195646_istek_gunlugu.sql:87-97` and `:113-123` — check-then-insert with no `on conflict`; a genuine double-tap race surfaces a raw `duplicate key` string. Faz 2c converted 23505 in `kullanici_adi_degistir` for exactly this reason.

**8.** `20260819211753_bag_kisileri.sql:17-21` — `bag_kisileri` accepts an unbounded `uuid[]` and returns `kullanici_adi`/`ad` for any id, ignoring `aramada_gorunsun`. Exposure matches the existing `baskasinin_profili`, so nothing new leaks, but the array should be capped (say 200) so one call cannot be turned into a bulk directory read.

**9.** Two of the three conversion paths have zero coverage anywhere. Live scenario 27 (`calistir.ts:814-849`) exercises only `check_inden_ayril`; the `pg_cron` body (`20260819194021…:655-662`) and `check_in_yap`'s close-of-previous-check-in (`:606-611`) are untested by both Jest and the live suite. The cron body is a string literal that is only parsed when it runs.

**10.** `lib/checkin.ts:44-49` changed `checkInYap` from `(mekanId, {lat,lng}, …)` to positional `(mekanId, lat, lng, …)`, while the live-test helper of the same name at `calistir.ts:49-55` uses a third order `(istemci, mekanId, lat, lng, bulunurluk)`. Three same-named shapes of interchangeable primitives; a swap compiles silently. The signature change earned nothing.

**11.** `src/app/index.tsx:15-19` fetches the badge once on mount with `useEffect`. Return from `/baglar` after accepting requests and the count is stale. `useFocusEffect` fixes it and also covers deferred minor 14.

**12.** `lib/bag.ts:56-77` — `bagDurumunuGetir` reads only the outgoing direction, so a profile where the other person has already sent *you* a request still shows "Takip et"; pressing it creates a crossed pair of pending requests rather than accepting theirs.

### Triage of the 17 deferred minors

1. **`istek_gunlugu` never pruned** — ship as-is. 50 rows/user/day; add the cron job whenever the table is next touched.
2. **`bagDurumunuGetir` two sequential selects** — ship as-is. One extra round trip on a screen that already awaits three things.
3. **Non-atomic exists-then-insert** — fix before merge. Two lines, and Faz 2c already established that raw Postgres errors don't reach users. See finding 7.
4. **No data-conversion test uses `takipcilerim`** — ship as-is. Live scenario 27 drives `takipcilerim` through the real database, which is stronger evidence than a mocked unit test.
5. **"Herkese acik" option row never clicked in a test** — ship as-is. Same code path as the covered options.
6. **`aniGorunurluguDegistir` writes a literal union instead of importing `AniGorunurlugu`** — fix before merge. One-line import, its sibling already does it, and it is exactly the drift the exported type exists to prevent.
7. **"Gizli"/"Herkes gorsun" never clicked in tests** — upgrade and fix. Recorded as a coverage gap; it is the untested half of the widest widening path in the branch (finding 2). Add the test with that fix.
8. **No `accessibilityState={{selected}}` on the tier pickers** — ship as-is as recorded, but bundle it with finding 6 — both are "the user cannot tell which option is active", one visually and one for screen readers.
9. **Duplicated "Istek gonderildi" block** — disagree with the framing. The duplication is harmless; the problem is that both copies are an inert `View` where the cancel affordance belongs (finding 5). Fix it, for a different reason than recorded.
10. **Error-path tests don't assert RPC arguments** — ship as-is. The reasoning recorded is correct: the call site is unconditional and covered by the happy path.
11. **Five near-identical FlatList row bodies** — fix before merge, but not for duplication. The five-FlatList *structure* is the defect (finding 4); extracting a row component falls out of the restructure for free.
12. **`kimlikleriOku`'s five same-typed positional params** — ship as-is. Worth switching to an object argument next time the file is opened; four call sites were verified.
13. **`gelenIstekleriGetir` resolves full records just for `.length`** — ship as-is. Two extra RPC round trips on home-screen open; revisit if the home screen gets slow.
14. **No unmount guard in `index.tsx`** — ship as-is. Matches house style; the staleness in finding 11 is the more useful thing to fix in that block.
15. **Cleanup uses `console.error` instead of `esitMi`** — ship as-is, and I disagree it was worth recording. A cleanup failure should be loud but must not be counted as a scenario failure; `console.error` is right.
16. **Scenario 24 cannot distinguish "row deleted" from "row hidden by RLS"** — ship as-is. Agreed with the recorded reasoning: an anon-key client cannot do better, and scenario 28 covers the deletion directly.
17. **Un-block before scenario 19 sits outside the `senaryo()` wrapper** — ship as-is. Established house pattern, matches the block before scenario 11.

### What I could not verify

- **That `authenticated` actually retains table-level UPDATE on `check_inler`** (finding 1). The evidence is strong but circumstantial: `calistir.ts:166-167` performs a direct client update of `check_inler` and passes live, and `20260819123253_profiller_sutun_yetkileri.sql` only makes sense if the blanket grant exists by default in this project. `select has_table_privilege('authenticated', 'public.check_inler', 'update')`, or a single PATCH of `mekan_id` from a test account, would settle it in one query. I did not run either — the review is read-only and this is the shared live database.
- **That the five stacked FlatLists actually clip** (finding 4). It is a known-bad React Native pattern and the test suite cannot see layout, but confirming needs a real render at phone width.
- **That the `pg_cron` body executes cleanly** (finding 9). `cron.schedule` stores SQL as text; `select * from cron.job_run_details where jobname = 'check-in-suresi-dolanlari-aniya-cevir' order by start_time desc limit 5` would show whether it has run without error since the migration.</result>

## Claude _2026-08-20 05:16:55 UTC_

Final inceleme **gerçek bir kritik açık buldu** ve canlı veritabanında doğruladım.

**Açık şu:** `authenticated` rolü `check_inler` tablosunda tablo düzeyinde UPDATE yetkisine sahip — `mekan_id`, `konum`, `bitis_zamani`, `kullanici_adi` dahil. Faz 2c bu sütun kısıtlamasını yalnızca `profiller`'e uygulamış, `check_inler`'e hiç uygulanmamış.

Bu fazın açtığı yeni politika, canlı check-in okumasını `gizli.ayni_mekanda_canli_mi(mekan_id)` fonksiyonuna bağlıyor — ve o fonksiyon yalnızca *"çağıranın o mekanda canlı bir satırı var mı"* diye soruyor. Yani bir kullanıcı bir kez gerçek check-in yapıp sonra **kendi satırının `mekan_id`'sini herhangi bir mekana çevirir** ve `bitis_zamani`'ni ileri atarsa, o mekandaki bütün açık check-in'leri **oraya hiç gitmeden ve süresiz** okur. Sızan şey de "şu mekandaydı" değil: `lib/checkin.ts` `konum` sütununu da seçiyor ve oraya cihazın GPS noktası yazılıyor.

Bunu üç ayrı sorguyla doğruladım: yetki var, politika gerçekten o fonksiyonu çağırıyor, ve fonksiyon istemcinin yazabildiği alana bakıyor. İstismar teorik değil.

Bu, tek tek görev incelemelerinin **yapısal olarak göremeyeceği** türden bir bulgu — açık diff'in içinde değil, diff'in dayandığı önceden var olan bir yetkide. Fazın son incelemesini yapmanın karşılığı tam olarak buydu.

Dokuz maddelik tek bir düzeltme dalgası gönderdim. İki maddeyi bilerek **dışarıda bıraktım** ve kaydettim: gönderilmiş bir sohbet isteğini geri çekme yolu olmaması, ve karşı taraf sana istek göndermişse profilde yine "Takip et" görünmesi. İkisi de kırık bir şey değil, **eksik özellik** — ne spec ne plan istiyor. Faz kapanışında gözden geçirilmemiş yüzey açmak yerine sana getireceğim; karar senin.

- Migration `20260820052327_istek_gonder_cift_dokunma.sql`: wraps the insert in `takip_istegi_gonder`/`sohbet_istegi_gonder` to convert `unique_violation` into a friendly message (verified live under a concurrent double-tap: one call succeeds, the other gets `Istegin zaten gonderilmis`, no raw Postgres error).

## Claude _2026-08-20 05:40:48 UTC_

**Kritik açık kapandı ve canlıda doğruladım:**

| Kontrol | Önce | Sonra |
|---|---|---|
| `check_inler` UPDATE yetkisi | ✗ açık | **kaldırıldı** |
| `mekan_id` / `konum` / `gorunurluk` sütun yetkisi | ✗ açık | **kaldırıldı** |
| `select` / `insert` / `delete` | duruyor | duruyor |

`delete`'in durması doğru — anı silme onu kullanıyor. Yeni `ani_gorunurlugunu_ayarla` RPC'si de yerinde: her satırı kendi `bulunurluk` değerine göre kelepçeliyor ve `konum is null` koşuluyla yalnızca **anılara** dokunuyor, canlı check-in'lere değil.

Kimsenin istemediği bir kontrolü de yaptım, aynı sınıftan olabilir diye: `check_inler`'de **INSERT politikası hiç yok**. RLS açık olduğu için istemci tablo yetkisine sahip olsa da ekleme yapamıyor — tek ekleme yolu `check_in_yap`. Yani kritik bulgunun kardeşi olabilecek "sahte satır ekle" yolu zaten kapalıymış.

Sayılar: jest 216 → **224**, `test:sema` 40 → **42**, `test:gorunurluk` 79 → **82** (sıfır düşme), `tsc` değişmedi.

Son kapı olarak kapsamlı yeniden incelemeyi gönderdim — yine en yetenekli modelle. Ona sorduğum en önemli şey şu: **kritiği kapatmak meşru bir yazma yolunu kırdı mı?** UPDATE tamamen kaldırıldığı için, o tabloyu daha önce güncelleyen her yerin artık RPC'den geçmesi lazım — hem uygulama kodunda hem canlı test koşucusunda. Kaçırılan bir tanesi, mock'lu Jest paketinin **göremeyeceği** bir çalışma zamanı hatası olurdu.

- **1.
