Controller's live check confirms the privilege is now false table-wide and per-column. New file, no existing migration edited.
- **2. IMPORTANT — settings republished hidden memories** — **ADDRESSED**. RPC at `20260820052249_check_inler_sutun_yetkileri.sql:42-63`: `auth.uid() is null` first, value whitelist, `set gorunurluk = bag.ani_gorunurlugu(bulunurluk, p_deger) where kullanici_id = auth.uid() and konum is null`, `revoke ... from public, anon` + `grant ... to authenticated`. Client moved at `mobil/lib/ayarlar.ts:76-85` (exported signature unchanged). Harness moved at `mobil/gorunurluk-testleri/calistir.ts:162-168`. New end-to-end scenario 31 at `calistir.ts:946-1001`.
- **3. IMPORTANT — check-in screen failed open** — **ADDRESSED**. `mobil/src/app/check-in/[mekanId].tsx:33` initial state is `Bulunurluk | null = null`; `:45-51` the `.catch(() =&gt; {})` is replaced by `setBulunurluk('gizli')` (guarded by the manual-change ref); `:79` early return in `checkInYapButonu`; `:166-171` `disabled={gonderiliyor || bulunurluk === null}`. Two new tests at `__tests__/ekranlar/check-in/[mekanId].test.tsx:133-168`.
- **4. IMPORTANT — five FlatLists in a non-scrolling View** — **ADDRESSED**, and exactly as scoped (no SectionList). `mobil/src/app/baglar.tsx:2` imports `ScrollView`; `:124` root becomes `&lt;ScrollView testID="baglar-kaydirici" style={stiller.kaydirici} contentContainerStyle={stiller.icerik}&gt;`; `scrollEnabled={false}` on all five lists (`:130, :157, :184, :201, :225`); `:241` close tag; new styles `kaydirici`/`icerik` at `:250-251`. I verified against `node_modules/@react-native/virtualized-lists/Lists/VirtualizedList.js:1162` that the nesting warning is gated on `this.props.scrollEnabled !== false`, so this is the RN-sanctioned pattern and produces no dev-console error.
- **5. IMPORTANT — unreadable selected chip** — **ADDRESSED**. `mobil/src/app/profil/ayarlar.tsx:233` adds `butonMetniSecili: { color: '#fff' }` (white on `#111` ≈ 19:1); applied to the default-visibility trio at `:154-176` and to the memory trio at `:187-211`, which now also has a selected state driven by `aniGorunurluk` (`:29`) plus accessibility labels on both trios.
- **6. MINOR — unbounded `uuid[]`** — **ADDRESSED**. `20260820052324_bag_kisileri_tavan.sql:16-18` raises `'Cok fazla kimlik'` above 200. I diffed the body against `20260819211753_bag_kisileri.sql`: identical except the cap. Empty/NULL arrays make `array_length` NULL, so the guard is a no-op there — correct.
- **7. MINOR — check-then-insert race** — **ADDRESSED**. `20260820052327_istek_gonder_cift_dokunma.sql:20-26` and `:51-57`. I diffed both bodies against `20260819195646_istek_gunlugu.sql`: byte-for-byte identical apart from the wrapper. Both tables have composite PKs (`takipler.sql:11`, `sohbet_istekleri.sql:10`), so `unique_violation` really fires.
- **8. MINOR — inlined union type** — **ADDRESSED**. `mobil/src/app/profil/ayarlar.tsx:11` imports `AniGorunurlugu`; `:108` uses it as the parameter type; the new `ANI_GORUNURLUK_SECENEKLERI` table at `:25-29` is typed with it too.
- **9. MINOR — stale badge** — **ADDRESSED**. `mobil/src/app/index.tsx:1-3, 15-21` uses `useFocusEffect(useCallback(..., []))`. I confirmed `useFocusEffect` is a real expo-router export (`node_modules/expo-router/build/exports.d.ts:19`), single-argument form as required.

### Named-risk checks

1. **Broken legitimate write path.** I enumerated every `from('check_inler')` in the repo (app, lib, harness, plus `araclar/`): `lib/checkin.ts:81/91/105` are selects, `:119` is a delete (privilege retained); `gorunurluk-testleri/calistir.ts:68/78/92/825/843/967/984` are all selects; `yardimcilar.ts:136` is a delete; `sema-dogrula.ts:275/287` are the two deliberate deny probes. The one migrated site was `calistir.ts` scenario 4. `lib/checkin.test.ts` only asserts select/delete. Every server-side write (`check_in_yap`, `check_inden_ayril`, the pg_cron job) is `security definer`/owner-run, so the revoke does not touch them — and scenario 31 exercises `check_inden_ayril` live and passed. No missed write path.
2. **Clamp in every direction.** `bag/ani_gorunurlugu` (`20260819194021_ani_gorunurlugu_yardimcisi.sql:11-23`) is `gizli -&gt; 'kimse'` unconditionally, `takipcilerim + herkese_acik -&gt; 'takipcilerim'`, else pass-through — so narrowing (`kimse`) still narrows in every branch. The RPC calls it per row inside the `update`, so each row is clamped by its own `bulunurluk`. `konum is null` confines it to memories; `kullanici_id = auth.uid()` confines it to the caller. Scenario 31 proves the `gizli` case end-to-end against the live DB.
3. **Weakened assertions.** I accounted for all 47 removed lines in the diff. Only two test files lost lines: `index.test.tsx` (one import line, re-added with `act`) and `lib/ayarlar.test.ts` (the three old `.from/.update/.eq` assertions, replaced by an RPC assertion **plus** `expect(supabase.from).not.toHaveBeenCalled()` **plus** a new throw-on-error test). `calistir.ts` scenario 4 lost the direct update and its error check, but both were replaced by an RPC call with the same `if (error) throw`, and the scenario's two `esitMi` assertions are unchanged. Nothing loosened, no new `try`/`catch` swallowing. Net: assertions strengthened.
4. **Item 7's handler scope.** The `begin ... exception ... end` block wraps *only* the single insert into `takipler` / `sohbet_istekleri`; it catches *only* `unique_violation`. `bag.istek_on_kontrol`, the `exists` pre-check and the `istek_gunlugu` insert all sit outside the block, so their errors propagate untouched.
5. **Item 3's fail-safe reachability.** The narrow value is set in the `catch` and the widest value is never in state before resolution (initial is `null`). The button is disabled by prop and by an in-handler guard. I ran the suite myself: `__tests__/ekranlar/check-in/[mekanId].test.tsx` passes, including the pending-promise test that presses before resolution and asserts `checkInYap` was not called.
6. **Scope discipline.** The 15 changed files contain neither `lib/bag.ts` nor `src/app/profil/[id].tsx`; `bagDurumunuGetir` is untouched and no withdraw-request RPC or UI exists in the diff. No deferred minors were picked up.

Independent run of the five touched Jest suites: 5 suites / 54 tests passed. Added lines are pure ASCII (`grep -P '[^\x00-\x7F]'` over `^+` lines: no matches), and all three new migrations start `2d 2d 20` — no BOM.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

**Minor** — `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820052249_check_inler_sutun_yetkileri.sql:48`: `if p_deger not in ('herkese_acik','takipcilerim','kimse')` evaluates to NULL when `p_deger` is NULL, so the validation guard is skipped. A hand-rolled `rpc('ani_gorunurlugunu_ayarla', { p_deger: null })` then reaches the update, where a `herkese_acik`/`takipcilerim` row would be set to NULL and hit the `not null` on `check_inler.gorunurluk` (`20260815001911_check_inler.sql:10`). Result is an aborted transaction with a raw `23502` instead of the friendly `'Gecersiz gorunurluk degeri'` — no partial write, no widening, no privacy impact. It also mirrors the pre-existing pattern in `check_in_yap`, so it is a consistency nit rather than a regression. `p_deger is null or p_deger not in (...)` would close it.

Nothing else. No Critical or Important breakage.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- The now-dead `"kendi check-in'ini guncelleyebilir"` UPDATE policy on `check_inler`: I would drop it in a later migration. It reads as "users can update their own check-ins", which is exactly the belief this wave disproved, and a future reader restoring a column grant on that basis would reopen the Critical. Non-blocking — it grants nothing while the privilege is gone.
- `src/app/profil/ayarlar.tsx` shows the memory-visibility chip as selected on the *requested* value, but the RPC may have clamped some rows below it (a `gizli`-origin memory stays `kimse` while "Herkes gorsun" appears selected). The bulk-action semantics make a truthful single value impossible, so this is inherent to the control, not to the fix; a one-line hint under the row would remove the ambiguity.
- Pre-existing non-ASCII outside the diff, untouched and correctly left alone: `gorunurluk-testleri/calistir.ts:88` (`ör.`) and `src/app/check-in/[mekanId].tsx:94` (em dash).

### Verdict

**Fix wave:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.
**Ready to merge:** yes — the location-privacy hole is closed at the privilege level with the only remaining write path being a server-side clamped RPC, every legitimate writer was migrated and proven live, and no assertion was weakened to get there.</result>

İş gönderildi. İki maddeyi tek migrasyonda kapatıyoruz:

**1. Ölü kalan UPDATE politikası düşürülüyor.** Bu politika artık hiçbir şey vermiyor (yetki geri alındı), ama *"kullanıcılar kendi check-in'lerini güncelleyebilir"* diye okunuyor — yani final incelemenin çürüttüğü inancın ta kendisi. İleride biri ona bakıp "demek ki serbest" diyerek sütun yetkisini geri verirse, kapattığımız kritik konum açığı yeniden açılır. Düşürmek ayrıca daha güvenli bir varsayılan bırakıyor: RLS açık ve UPDATE politikası yoksa, biri yanlışlıkla yetki verse bile güncelleme yine reddedilir.

**2. NULL, geçersiz-değer korumasını atlıyor.** SQL'de `x not in ('a','b')` ifadesi `x` NULL olduğunda `true` değil **NULL** döner — yani `if` gövdesi hiç çalışmaz, doğrulama sessizce atlanır ve kullanıcıya Türkçe mesaj yerine ham Postgres hatası gider.

Bunu tek örnek olarak değil **sınıf olarak** kapattırıyorum: canlı veritabanında üç örneğini buldum — `ani_gorunurlugunu_ayarla`, `check_in_yap` ve `sikayet_gonder`. Sonuncusu Faz 2b'den geliyor, yani bu turda hiç dokunmadığımız bir yer; sınıfı aramasaydım görünmezdi.

`sikayet_gonder`'in `p_sebep` ve `p_hedef_id` parametrelerinde ise hiç doğrulama yok — aynı belirtiyi verirler ama düzeltilecek mevcut bir kontrol olmadığı için onları kapsam dışı bıraktım; rapora not düşülecek. Kapsamı genişletmek yerine ayrı bir iş olarak duracak.

Uygulayıcı `test:gorunurluk`'ta 11 düşme gördü ve "oturumdan saatler önceki önceden var olan durum" dedi. Bu yanlıştı: en eski satır 04:22, yani benim temizlediğim andan **sonra**. Gerçek sebep daha önemli —

**Test paketi kendi kendini zehirliyor.** `test:gorunurluk`, senaryolarında gerçek takip/sohbet isteği gönderiyor ve bunlar günlük 50 istek tavanından düşüyor. Tavan ekle-only bir günlüğü sayıyor (Görev 9'daki kararım) ve istemci o satırları **tasarım gereği silemiyor**. Yani her koşum kotadan yiyor: üç saatte on koşum yapılmış, hesap 50'ye dayanmış, paket kendi kendine kırılmış.

Ölçüm bunu net gösteriyor — on ayrı dakika kümesi, her biri bir koşum:

```
04:22 → 4    04:39 → 2    05:34 → 5
04:23 → 8    04:43 → 10   05:35 → 1
04:37 → 4    04:49 → 5    07:28 → 3
04:38 → 8
```

Yani paket günde ancak ~8 kez çalıştırılabiliyor, sonra yanlış alarm vermeye başlıyor. Bu, gelecekte birinin gerçek bir kırılmayı "kota bitmiştir" diye geçmesine ya da tersine olmayan bir kusuru kovalamasına yol açar. Kotayı temizleyip paketi yeniden koştum: **82 iddia, hepsi geçiyor** — yani düzeltme temiz.

<result>Both are single-signature `create or replace` (not distinct overloads), confirming the later migration (20260819194021) genuinely supersedes the earlier one for the same function signature — implementer correctly used the later source.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant. Both required items are present, correctly scoped, and nothing extra was added.
- Item 1 (drop dead UPDATE policy): exact statement from the brief, `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820060000_null_korumasi_ve_olu_politika.sql:12`.
- Item 2 (NULL guard, 3 functions): `is null or` added to exactly the specified `if` conditions, bodies otherwise copied from the correct source migrations, original error messages preserved (`ani_gorunurlugunu_ayarla:109-111`, `check_in_yap:147-149`, `sikayet_gonder:207-209`).
- Out-of-scope item honored: `sikayet_gonder`'s `p_sebep`/`p_hedef_id` left unvalidated, only noted in a comment (`...sql:190-191`) — no scope creep.
- New test assertions added per instructions, in the same file/pattern (`mobil/gorunurluk-testleri/sema-dogrula.ts:290-329`), based on an actually-observed error shape rather than an assumed one (report documents the observation step).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff alone: whether `supabase db push` was truly applied to the live remote (I did not re-run it; controller's independent re-run of `test:gorunurluk` at 82/82 is consistent with the migration being live, so I treat this as effectively corroborated, not purely trust-the-report).

### Named-risk checks

1. **Transcription fidelity** — Diffed each of the three new function bodies line-by-line against their stated source files (`20260820052249_check_inler_sutun_yetkileri.sql`, `20260819194021_ani_gorunurlugu_yardimcisi.sql`, `20260816114002_sikayet_rpc.sql`). All three are byte-identical to source except the single intended `if` line. Auth guard, `set search_path = public`, insert/update column lists, and the `revoke`/`grant` pairs are unchanged in all three. Confirmed correct source was used for `check_in_yap` (`20260819194021`, not the superseded `20260819190832` — verified both are single, non-overloaded `create or replace` definitions of the same signature, so the later one really does supersede). Implementer's fidelity claim holds.

2. **Dropping the dead UPDATE policy** — Grepped all migrations for `check_inler` policy DDL. The delete policy `"kendi anisini silebilir"` was created once (`20260815001911_check_inler.sql:33`) and never referenced again outside its own file. The select policy `"check-in gorunurlugu"` has its own independent drop/recreate history confined to earlier migrations (`20260816130000`, `20260816140000`, `20260816180000`, `20260819185621`) and is not touched here. The new migration's only policy statement is the one `drop policy "kendi check-in'ini guncelleyebilir"` (created in `20260816180000_check_inler_recursion_ve_guncelleme.sql:53`). Confirmed: only the dead UPDATE policy is dropped.

3. **NULL guard logic** — `p_deger is null or p_deger not in (...)` (and the analogous forms) correctly short-circuits on NULL via `or`, so NULL now hits the same `raise exception` as before, before reaching the not-null column. Same non-NULL values remain rejected since the second operand of the `or` is byte-identical to the original condition. Each function retained its own message (`'Gecersiz gorunurluk degeri'`, `'Gecersiz bulunurluk degeri'`, `'Gecersiz sikayet hedefi'`) — no unified message.

4. **New test assertions** — Real observed behaviour, not assumed (report shows an explicit observation script run before writing assertions, and the values match code exactly). All three pairs would fail if their guard were reverted, but not always via the mechanism the accompanying comment implies:
   - `ani_gorunurlugunu_ayarla` and `sikayet_gonder`: removing the guard changes both the resulting code and/or message in this test's exact scenario, so both assertions are discriminating.
   - `check_in_yap` (`sema-dogrula.ts:301-311`): the test passes a nonexistent `p_mekan_id` (`00000000-...-000000000000`). If the guard were reverted, execution would fall through to the mekan lookup and raise `'Mekan bulunamadi'` — still code `P0001`. So the `esitMi(checkinNullHatasi?.code, 'P0001', ...)` assertion (comment: "ham 23502 degil") would pass regardless of whether the guard exists; only the paired message assertion (`'Gecersiz bulunurluk degeri'` vs `'Mekan bulunamadi'`) actually catches a reverted guard. Net effect is still a valid regression test (the pair together fails on regression), but the code-check's own justifying comment is not accurate for this specific function — see Minor findings.

### Strengths
- Faithful, minimal-diff transcription of three `security definer` functions under real time pressure to get exactly one line right in each — verified independently and it holds.
- Correctly identified and used the superseded-vs-current source file for `check_in_yap`, exactly as flagged as a landmine in the brief.
- Migration is additive-only (`create or replace` + one `drop policy`), never edits an already-applied file, and is the latest-timestamped migration in the directory.
- Test assertions were written from an actual observed error shape (code + message) rather than assumed, per instructions, and the throwaway observation script was deleted afterward.
- Both changed files are UTF-8 without BOM and pass an ASCII-only scan; I independently re-ran the ASCII/BOM checks rather than trusting the report's claim, and they hold.
- Correctly diagnosed `sikayet_gonder`'s missing `p_sebep`/`p_hedef_id` validation as out of scope and did not touch it, resisting scope creep even though it's a related, tempting fix.
- Report's central diagnosis of the `test:gorunurluk` failures (pre-existing quota exhaustion, not a regression) was independently investigated with a live SQL query showing the account pinned at exactly 50/50 with the oldest row predating the session — a correct and well-evidenced diagnosis, unlike its counterpart claim about which the controller found the implementer's diagnosis wrong (here the implementer's own diagnosis is right and better-evidenced than the controller's later blanket correction implies was needed).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `sema-dogrula.ts:301-303`: the `check_in_yap(null)` code-equality assertion's comment ("ham 23502 degil") implies it is testing the guard's effect on the error code, but with the dummy `p_mekan_id`, the code is `P0001` either way (guard present -&gt; `'Gecersiz bulunurluk degeri'`, guard reverted -&gt; `'Mekan bulunamadi'` also `P0001`). The regression-catching power for this function comes entirely from the paired message assertion. Not a functional bug — the test still fails on regression — but worth a one-line comment fix so a future reader doesn't over-trust the code check in isolation for this function.
- `docs/faz3a-takip-isleri.md` section 3 and the brief both call the dropped-policy comment logic out as important; consider, in a future pass, adding a regression test asserting that UPDATE on `check_inler` is rejected end-to-end (privilege + no policy), since this migration removes the last policy that a reader might otherwise assume enforces it — currently only covered indirectly by the pre-existing `mekan_id` privilege test at `sema-dogrula.ts:14-23`, not by anything specific to this migration's diff.
- Harness design note (not a finding against this change, per the controller's framing): `npm run test:gorunurluk` spends real, non-resettable quota (up to 50 rows/day) from the same test account on every run, with no client-side cleanup path. Running it more than ~2x/day self-DoSes future reviewers exactly as it did here. Worth a `--dry-run`/mock mode or an admin cleanup script for the next person who hits this.

### Assessment

**Change quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** Both requested items are implemented exactly as specified, the security-definer contract is intact in all three re-created functions, the dropped policy is confirmed dead and cleanly isolated from the live select/delete policies, and the new test assertions are grounded in observed behaviour and do detect a reverted guard. The only issue found is a cosmetic mismatch between one assertion's justifying comment and what it actually discriminates on — not a functional defect.</result>

## Claude _2026-08-20 07:59:04 UTC_

Plan hazır, üç göreve böldüm ve sunucu tarafı çalışmaya başladı.

**Görev 1 (sunucu):** sohbet isteğini geri çekme RPC'si, `sikayet_gonder`'in kalan iki parametresine doğrulama, ve `istek_gunlugu` için günlük budama işi.

### Named-risk checks

1. **Quota-refund trap** — Checked. `sohbet_istegini_geri_cek` body (`.superpowers/aciklar-g1.diff:136-159`) contains a single `delete from public.sohbet_istekleri ... ; if not found then raise exception ...` and zero references to `istek_gunlugu`. Grepped the whole migration file for `istek_gunlugu`: it appears only in the cron-job section (item 3), never inside the RPC. Trap avoided.

2. **Withdraw scope** — Checked. `delete from public.sohbet_istekleri where gonderen_id = auth.uid() and alan_id = p_kullanici_id and durum = 'beklemede'` (diff:150-153). `gonderen_id = auth.uid()` means the caller can only be the sender, never the recipient of a row it deletes; `durum = 'beklemede'` excludes accepted rows; `alan_id = p_kullanici_id` scopes to one target. Correct on all three axes.

3. **`sikayet_gonder` transcription fidelity** — Verified by direct diff against `20260820060000_null_korumasi_ve_olu_politika.sql:117-146` (read in full, not taken on the report's word). Auth guard, `p_hedef_tur is null or ... not in (...)` guard, self-report check, insert column list, and revoke/grant lines are byte-for-byte identical. The only delta is two new `if` blocks inserted between the `p_hedef_tur` guard and the self-report check. Ordering is correct: the `p_hedef_id is null` check runs *before* the self-report comparison (`p_hedef_id = auth.uid()`), which matters — a null `p_hedef_id` would otherwise make that comparison evaluate to `null` (not `true`), silently skipping the self-check and falling through to the `not null` insert failure this task exists to prevent.

4. **Pruning window** — Checked against the cap's own query. `20260819195646_istek_gunlugu.sql:54` counts the cap with `olusturuldu &gt; now() - interval '1 day'`. The new cron body deletes `olusturuldu &lt; now() - interval '2 days'` (diff:221), a full day of safety margin past the 1-day window, matching the plan's stated reasoning. Job name `istek-gunlugu-buda` is unique in the migrations tree (grepped), so it can't collide with or silently replace an unrelated existing job.

5. **New assertions** — Checked each for real-behavior sensitivity, not fixture assumptions:
   - `anonGeriCekHatasi?.code === '42501'` (diff `sema-dogrula.ts:44-48`): would fail if the `revoke ... from anon` grant were dropped (call would instead reach the function and either succeed or raise `P0001`), so it does test the guard, though it exercises the SQL-grant layer rather than the in-body `auth.uid() is null` check — an acceptable substitute since revoke makes the body unreachable by an anon key, and the plan only asked for "rejected," not which layer.
   - `sebepNullHatasi`/`hedefIdNullHatasi` assertions (diff `sema-dogrula.ts:53-75`) check both `code === 'P0001'` and the exact Turkish message text, using a live `bId` from `ikiKullaniciIleBaglan()` rather than a hardcoded id — would fail if either new `if` guard were removed (raw `23502` would surface instead) or if the message text drifted. Real, non-tautological tests, matching the plan's "observe first, then assert" instruction (report shows an intermediate `console.log(JSON.stringify(error))` observation step before the final assertions were written).

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant — 2a and 2b both implemented per spec, no scope creep into `src/app/`, no extra follow-request withdraw RPC wrapper (correctly deferred to `takibiBirak` per binding decision 1).

### Named-risk checks

1. **Column direction (the core risk).** Verified all four queries in `mobil/lib/bag.ts:69-93` directly against the migrations I read:
   - `takipler` (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819183239_takipler.sql:7-8`): `takip_eden_id`, `takip_edilen_id`.
   - `sohbet_istekleri` (`.../20260819183711_sohbet_istekleri.sql:6-7`): `gonderen_id`, `alan_id`.
   - `takip` (giden): `.eq('takip_eden_id', benimId).eq('takip_edilen_id', kullaniciId)` — correct (`bag.ts:73-74`).
   - `sohbet` (giden): `.eq('gonderen_id', benimId).eq('alan_id', kullaniciId)` — correct (`bag.ts:79-80`).
   - `gelenTakip`: `.eq('takip_eden_id', kullaniciId).eq('takip_edilen_id', benimId)` — correct, the other user is the follower (`bag.ts:85-86`).
   - `gelenSohbet`: `.eq('gonderen_id', kullaniciId).eq('alan_id', benimId)` — correct, the other user is the sender (`bag.ts:91-92`).
   No swap present. Conclusion: correct.

2. **Existing contract unchanged.** `takip`/`sohbet` fields, `BagDurumu` type, and query semantics for the original two queries are byte-for-byte identical to the pre-diff logic, just moved into `Promise.all` (`bag.ts:69-80` vs. old sequential `await` calls in the diff's `-` lines). Return type is additive only (`bag.ts:61-66`). Conclusion: no shift.

3. **Test sourcing.** All expected RPC/column names in `mobil/lib/bag.test.ts` are hardcoded string literals (`'takip_eden_id'`, `'sohbet_istegini_geri_cek'`, etc.), not imported or derived from `bag.ts`. I independently confirmed the RPC name, parameter name (`p_kullanici_id`) and error message (`'Geri cekilecek istek bulunamadi'`) against `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820070000_..._budama.sql:14,34-38`, exact match. The four directional tests (`bag.test.ts:215-280`) use `izleyenZincirOlustur` to record the literal `(column, value)` pairs passed to `.eq()` and assert on them directly, so a column swap in `bag.ts` changes what's recorded and fails the assertion — this is a real behavioral test, not tautological. Conclusion: sound.

4. **`Promise.all` error behaviour.** `bag.ts:69-99`: four queries run concurrently, then four independent `if (x.error) throw` checks run sequentially after all settle. No swallowing: Supabase's `.maybeSingle()` resolves with `{data, error}` rather than rejecting for query-level errors, so every error is checked; if any one of the four promises truly rejected (network failure), `Promise.all` itself would reject and propagate as a thrown error since there is no surrounding try/catch. No partial result is ever returned — the function either returns all four fields or throws before returning. Conclusion: correct, consistent with the pre-existing single-error-surfacing pattern.

- Column-direction risk mitigated by an actual argument-capturing mock (`izleyenZincirOlustur`, `bag.test.ts:45-62`) rather than just checking return values — this is the right test shape for this specific risk, and the report's manual mutation test (swap `gelenTakip` columns, confirm exactly the directional test fails) is a credible, verifiable claim given the test design.

<result>### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant on the letter of the plan: 3a (outgoing-pending "Istegi geri cek" button + incoming "Kabul et"/"Reddet" block with byte-identical caption), 3b (giden istekler "Geri cek" buttons wired to the correct RPC per type), 3c (ayarlar.tsx explanatory note) are all present and match the plan's wording and RPC routing.
- ❌ Issues found: none that break the plan's literal text, but see two Important findings below that are direct, foreseeable consequences of the new code this diff adds (not pre-existing Gorev 1/2 debt).
- No Extra: diff touches only `mobil/src/app/{baglar.tsx,kullanici/[id].tsx,profil/ayarlar.tsx}` and their tests — nothing in `mobil/supabase/migrations/` or `mobil/lib/`.

### Named-risk checks
1. **Direction.** Traced every branch in `kullanici/[id].tsx`: `takip === 'beklemede'` / `sohbet === 'beklemede'` (outgoing) → "Istegi geri cek" → `takibiBirakEt`/`sohbetIsteginiGeriCekEt` (lines 656-682). `gelenTakip === 'beklemede'` / `gelenSohbet === 'beklemede'` (incoming) → "Kabul et"/"Reddet" → `takipIstegineYanitVer`/`sohbetIstegineYanitVer` (lines 690-715). No crossed wires. `baglar.tsx`'s "Giden istekler" list only ever offers "Geri cek" (line 512-521), never Yanitla. Correct.
2. **Withdraw wiring.** `kullanici/[id].tsx:602-610` sohbet withdraw calls `sohbetIsteginiGeriCek`; the existing `takibiBirakEt` (reused, unchanged) is wired to takip withdraw at line 660. `baglar.tsx:458-476`: `gidenTakipIsteginiGeriCekEt` → `takibiBirak`, `gidenSohbetIsteginiGeriCekEt` → `sohbetIsteginiGeriCek`. Both correct.
3. **Optimistic-update discipline.** All four new handlers (`kullanici/[id].tsx:602-634`) and both new `baglar.tsx` handlers (`baglar.tsx:458-476`) call `await` first, mutate state only on success, and `setHata` without touching state on failure. Failure-path tests exist for: sohbet withdraw (`kullanici/[id].test.tsx:270-280`), takip accept (`:320-331`), takip withdraw via reused handler (pre-existing `:378`), and giden-takip withdraw (`baglar.test.tsx:95-107`). Gap: no explicit failure test for `sohbetIstegineYanitVer` (incoming chat accept/reject) or for the giden-sohbet withdraw path — same code pattern as the tested siblings, so low risk, but coverage is asymmetric (Minor).
4. **Async testing-library usage.** Checked every new `render`/`fireEvent` call in both test files — all are `await`ed, no violations found.
5. **Recipient-only semantics.** Confirmed no path lets the sender invoke `takipIsteginiYanitla`/`sohbetIsteginiYanitla`: those calls only appear inside the `gelenTakip`/`gelenSohbet` JSX blocks, never under `takip`/`sohbet`.

### The implementer's two flagged design calls
1. **"Istegi geri cek" wording** — not really a discretionary call, it's the literal string the plan specifies (`aciklar-plan.md:131`, "'Istegi geri cek' yazan bir Pressable"). Correctly implemented and consistent across both screens. No objection.
2. **Single tagged `FlatList` for "Giden istekler"** — reasonable given the pre-existing "one scroller" test constraint, but the execution has a real bug: `keyExtractor={(k) =&gt; k.id}` (`baglar.tsx`, unchanged line near 504) is unchanged even though the list now merges two arrays that can legitimately share the same `id` (a user can have a pending outgoing takip request **and** a pending outgoing sohbet request to the same person at once — both buttons are independently offered on the profile screen). React will see duplicate keys, log a warning, and risk misapplying reconciliation across rows on the next state update (e.g. withdrawing one of the two requests). This existed in the pre-diff array-concat too, but back then rows were inert text with identical content, so a collision was cosmetically harmless; this diff turns rows into distinct interactive buttons bound to different handlers, which makes the same collision a functional correctness risk. Fix is a one-line composite key: `` `${item.id}-${item.tur}` ``. Not tested — both new tests use disjoint ids (`k3` for takip, `k6` for sohbet), so the collision path is untested.

### Strengths
- Clean, consistent pattern reuse across both screens (await-then-setState-else-setHata), matching the project's existing optimistic-update idiom exactly.
- Good type hygiene in `kullanici/[id].tsx:578-580`: dropped the stale local `{ takip; sohbet }` type in favor of `Awaited&lt;ReturnType&lt;typeof bagDurumunuGetir&gt;&gt;`, so the state type can't drift from the module's actual contract.
- Disclosure caption is scoped correctly — only attached to the takip accept block, not the sohbet block, which is semantically right (accepting a chat request has no bearing on check-in visibility).
- Solid direction/recipient-only correctness (risks 1 and 5) — the part of this task that mattered most given the app's stated core risk.
- Genuine TDD evidence in the report (failing-then-passing counts), and tests do exercise real button labels/RPC args/error paths rather than snapshotting internals.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **`baglar.tsx` giden-istekler key collision**: `keyExtractor={(k) =&gt; k.id}` on the merged takip+sohbet list (around `baglar.tsx:504`, list built at `baglar.tsx:500-503`) can produce duplicate keys when the same person has both a pending outgoing follow and a pending outgoing chat request, risking misreconciled rows on update. Fix: `keyExtractor={(item) =&gt; `${item.id}-${item.tur}`}`. Untested — add a test with one person appearing in both `gidenTakip` and `gidenSohbet`.
- **Post-accept "Sohbet acik" state doesn't account for `gelenSohbet`**: `kullanici/[id].tsx:670-687` derives the "Sohbet iste" / "Istegi geri cek" / "Sohbet acik" trio solely from `bagDurum.sohbet` (my outgoing direction). After a viewer accepts an *incoming* chat request via the new `sohbetIstegineYanitVer` handler (`:624-634`), `gelenSohbet` becomes `'kabul'` but `sohbet` stays `'yok'` (the viewer never sent their own request), so the profile re-renders showing "Sohbet iste" again — inviting the user to send a second request to someone with whom chat is already accepted. This exact path only became reachable because this diff adds the accept UI; before it, `gelenSohbet` could never reach `'kabul'` through the UI, so the gap was latent. No test catches it (the two new "gelen sohbet" success tests only assert the Kabul/Reddet block disappears, not that "Sohbet acik" subsequently appears). Suggest: `(bagDurum?.sohbet === 'kabul' || bagDurum?.gelenSohbet === 'kabul')` for the "Sohbet acik" branch, and gate "Sohbet iste" accordingly.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- Test coverage is asymmetric on the failure path: `sohbetIstegineYanitVer` (incoming chat accept/reject) and giden-sohbet withdraw in `baglar.tsx` have no dedicated failure-path test, unlike their takip counterparts. Code is correct by inspection (identical pattern to the tested siblings), but the "verify failure leaves state unchanged" guarantee isn't independently exercised for every new handler.
- `gelenIstekBlok`/`aciklama`/`kucukButon`/`kucukButonYazi` styles in `kullanici/[id].tsx:738-741` duplicate identically-valued styles already in `baglar.tsx`; consistent with this codebase's existing per-screen style duplication convention, so not a blocker, just an opportunity for a shared style module later.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** Core requirements (direction correctness, RPC wiring, optimistic-update discipline, async test hygiene, recipient-only gating, and the byte-identical disclosure caption) are all solid and well-tested. But two concrete, diff-introduced defects — the giden-istekler key collision and the "Sohbet acik" state not reflecting an accepted incoming chat request — are real correctness gaps in exactly the interactive surfaces this task added, and neither is caught by the existing tests.</result>

## Claude _2026-08-20 08:30:54 UTC_

İnceleme **iki gerçek kusur** buldu, ikisi de bu diff'in getirdiği:

**1. Anahtar çakışması.** Giden istekler listesi artık takip ve sohbet dizilerini birleştiriyor ama `keyExtractor` hâlâ sadece `id` kullanıyor. Aynı kişiye hem takip hem sohbet isteği gönderilebiliyor — profil ekranı ikisini bağımsız sunuyor — yani aynı id listede iki kez çıkabilir. Diff öncesi zararsızdı (satırlar hareketsiz metindi, ikisi aynı görünüyordu); bu diff satırları **farklı işleyicilere bağlı ayrı butonlara** çevirdi, dolayısıyla artık biri geri çekilince yanlış satır güncellenebilir.

**2. Kabul edilmiş gelen sohbet isteği profilde görünmüyor.** Sohbet üçlüsü yalnızca kullanıcının kendi giden yönüne bakıyor. Gelen bir isteği kabul edince `gelenSohbet` `kabul` oluyor ama `sohbet` `yok` kalıyor — profil yeniden çizilince **"Sohbet iste" gösteriyor**, yani sohbeti zaten açık olan birine ikinci istek göndermeye davet ediyor. Bu yol yalnızca kabul arayüzünü eklediğimiz için ulaşılabilir hale geldi.

Düzeltmeyi gönderirken bir tuzağı özellikle yasakladım: **aynı değişikliği takip tarafına yapmasın.** Takip tek yönlü — birinin beni takip etmesi benim onu takip ettiğim anlamına gelmez, dolayısıyla orada "Takip et" göstermek doğru davranış. Yalnızca sohbet simetrik. Bu düzeltmeyi yanlış yapmanın en olası yolu buydu.

Test added in `mobil/__tests__/ekranlar/baglar.test.tsx` ("ayni kisiye hem takip hem sohbet istegi..."): renders two `mert` rows, presses first "Geri cek", asserts `takibiBirak('k7')` called and `sohbetIsteginiGeriCek` NOT called, then presses the remaining row and asserts `sohbetIsteginiGeriCek('k7')` is called and the row fully disappears. Requirement met as literally specified.

- **2 (Important) — Accepted incoming chat request invisible** — ADDRESSED. `mobil/src/app/kullanici/[id].tsx:187-201`: "Sohbet acik" now gated on `bagDurum?.sohbet === 'kabul' || bagDurum?.gelenSohbet === 'kabul'`; "Sohbet iste" now gated on `sohbet === 'yok' &amp;&amp; gelenSohbet !== 'kabul' &amp;&amp; gelenSohbet !== 'beklemede'`. Two new tests in `mobil/__tests__/ekranlar/kullanici/[id].test.tsx` cover exactly the required scenarios (`gelenSohbet:'kabul', sohbet:'yok'` → "Sohbet acik" present / "Sohbet iste" absent; `gelenSohbet:'beklemede'` → "Sohbet iste" absent).

- **3 (Minor) — Asymmetric error-path coverage** — ADDRESSED. New tests: `baglar.test.tsx` "giden sohbet istegi geri cekme basarisiz olursa..." (line ~181 in diff) and `kullanici/[id].test.tsx` "gelen sohbet istegini yanitlama basarisiz olursa..." (line ~97 in diff). Both follow the sibling-test pattern: mock a rejection, assert the error text renders, and assert the row/block is unchanged (`getByText('deniz')` still present; `getByText('Kabul et')`/`getByText('Reddet')` still present).

### Named-risk checks

1. **Follow trio unchanged** — Confirmed from the diff: the only `-`/`+` lines in `kullanici/[id].tsx` are within the sohbet trio (lines 185-207 region) and no line containing `takip ===` or `gelenTakip ===` was touched; the takip block (lines 171-183 in the current file) is unchanged context. Agree with the implementer/controller.

2. **Does the fix cover every combination?** `BagDurumu` only has three real values (`yok`/`beklemede`/`kabul` — `lib/bag.ts:3`, and `durumOku` always coalesces null to `'yok'`, so `undefined` is not a reachable production state, only an artifact of older test mocks). Walking the 3×3 grid of `sohbet` × `gelenSohbet`:
   - `(yok, yok)` → "Sohbet iste". `(yok, kabul)` → "Sohbet acik". `(yok, beklemede)` → nothing in the trio, but the separate incoming "Kabul et"/"Reddet" block renders — correct, not a "renders nothing" bug.
   - `(beklemede, yok)` → "Istegi geri cek" only — correct.
   - `(beklemede, kabul)` → **"Istegi geri cek" AND "Sohbet acik" render simultaneously** — a real conflicting-controls combination. It's reachable in practice: two users cross-send chat requests, then the viewer accepts the incoming one (`sohbetIstegineYanitVer(true)`), which only flips `gelenSohbet` to `'kabul'` locally/server-side while the viewer's own outgoing `sohbet` stays `'beklemede'` (a separate DB row). Pressing "Istegi geri cek" here would still succeed server-side (not rejected), so it's a confusing double-control rather than a broken action. This gap is inherited directly from the instruction sheet's literal predicate (which only says when "Sohbet acik" and "Sohbet iste" should show, and says nothing about suppressing "Istegi geri cek"), not a deviation by the implementer — it also predates this fix round in weaker form (before the fix, this cell showed only "Istegi geri cek", silently wrong instead of doubly-shown).
   - `(kabul, *)` → always "Sohbet acik" only, for all three gelenSohbet values — no new conflict there (Acik was already true from `sohbet==='kabul'` alone).
   - No combination renders nothing overall, and no combination offers an action the server would reject.

3. **Do the new tests discriminate?**
   - Finding 2's tests: yes — both explicitly assert presence of "Sohbet acik" and absence of "Sohbet iste" via `queryByText(...).toBeNull()`, matching the risk's bar.
   - Finding 1's test: it satisfies the letter of the instruction (asserts the correct handler is called for each row and the correct row disappears), but there's a real question whether it would have failed against the pre-fix `keyExtractor={(k) =&gt; k.id}`. React's array reconciliation for a duplicate-key transition from 2 items → 1 item matches the surviving new child positionally/by-key against whichever old fiber the (colliding) key resolves to, then re-renders that fiber with the new item's own props — content is prop-driven, not identity-driven, for these stateless rows. Working through the specific sequence in this test (press row 0 → takip removed → single sohbet row remains), the remaining row's rendered text/handler would very likely still come out correct even under the old buggy extractor, because there's only one item driving `data` at that point. So this test is a reasonable "does the right thing happen" test and a good regression net for future changes, but it is not a strong, certain proof that the old code would have failed it — this is a soft caveat, not a basis to reopen the finding, since the underlying code defect (missing composite key) is objectively and correctly fixed regardless.

4. **Async usage / no weakened assertions** — All new `render`/`fireEvent` calls in both test files are awaited (`await render(...)`, `await fireEvent.press(...)`, `expect(await screen.findByText(...))`, `await waitFor(...)`). Diff stat confirms both test files are pure additions (`baglar.test.tsx +45/-0`, `kullanici/[id].test.tsx +36/-0`) — no existing test assertion was touched or removed. The only `-` lines in the whole diff are the `keyExtractor` line in `baglar.tsx` and the JSX restructuring of the sohbet trio in `kullanici/[id].tsx`, both intentional production-code replacements, not weakened tests.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
None. (The `(beklemede, kabul)` double-control case noted under named-risk #2 is a residual gap inherited from the instruction sheet's own predicate and a pre-existing crossed-request edge case, not a fresh regression introduced by sloppy implementation — flagged for the record, non-blocking.)

None. The deferred shared-style-module extraction was correctly left untouched in this diff.

## Bölüm 1 — Bağ modeli revizyonu (Faz 3a değişikliği)

**Şema değişmiyor.** `takipler` aynı kalıyor; değişen tek şey satırların ne zaman yazıldığı.

|---|---|---|
| İstek gönder | tek satır A→B `beklemede` | aynı |
| **Kabul** | A→B `kabul` | A→B `kabul` **+ B→A `kabul`** |

| Bağı kopar | yalnızca A→B silinir | **iki yön de** silinir |
| Engelle | iki yön de silinir | aynı (zaten doğru) |

Karşılıklılık tek bir yerde kuruluyor: kabul RPC'si. `takipler`'e yalnızca `security definer` RPC'ler yazabiliyor — insert/update politikası bilerek yok — dolayısıyla ikinci satırı atlayan bir yazma yolu mevcut değil.

- `takipciyi_cikar` ile `takibi_birak` aynı işe iniyor. `takibi_birak` kalıyor, diğeri düşürülüyor.

## Bölüm 2 — Mesajlaşma çekirdeği

**Üç yeni tablo** (üst spec'in veri modelinden):

```
konusmalar       (id, tur, birebir_anahtar, olusturuldu)
konusma_uyeleri  (konusma_id, kullanici_id, gizlendi_mi, son_okuma)
mesajlar         (id, konusma_id, gonderen_id, metin, olusturuldu)
```

`tur` şimdilik hep `birebir`; 3c'nin mekan odaları için duruyor. `birebir_anahtar` sıralı çiftten türeyen benzersiz bir anahtar (`en_kucuk_id + en_buyuk_id`) — bir çiftin **tek** konuşması olmasını yarışa açık olmayan biçimde garantiliyor. Aynı çift hem takipleşip hem sohbet isteğiyle bağlanmış olsa bile tek konuşma.

**Yazma kapısı tek bir fonksiyonda:** `bag.yazabilir_mi(hedef)` → engelli değilse **ve** (karşılıklı takip **veya** kabul edilmiş sohbet isteği) ise true.

Karşılıklı takip kontrolünde iki yönü de ayrı ayrı soracağım. Kabul zaten iki satırı da yazıyor, yani tek kontrol yeterli olurdu — ama başıboş bir tek yönlü satır (eski veri, ileride bir hata) o durumda sessizce "bağlı" sayılırdı. İki yön sormak bunu veri hatası olarak bırakır, güvenlik hatasına dönüştürmez.

**RPC'ler** — hepsi `security definer`, tabloya doğrudan yazma yolu yok:

| RPC | İş |
|---|---|
| `mesaj_gonder(kullanici_id, metin)` | Kapıyı kontrol eder, konuşmayı bulur-ya-oluşturur, mesajı yazar, iki taraf için de gizliliği kaldırır |
| `konusmalarim()` | Mesaj kutusu: karşı kişi, son mesaj, okunmamış sayısı, **şu an yazılabilir mi** |
| `mesajlari_getir(konusma_id, ...)` | Sayfalı geçmiş |
| `konusmayi_okundu_isaretle(konusma_id)` | `son_okuma`'yı ilerletir |
| `konusmayi_gizle(konusma_id)` | Yalnızca çağıranın tarafından gizler |

Gizliliğin `mesaj_gonder` tarafından kaldırılması, senin seçtiğin davranışı kuran şey: A siler, B yazar, konuşma A'da geri gelir.

**Bağ koptuğunda** yazma kapısı `false` döner ama geçmiş okunur kalır — `konusmalarim()` bunu satır başına bildirir, ekran da yazma alanını kapatır. Yetki her gönderimde sunucuda ölçülüyor, konuşma açılırken bir kez değil.

**Sessiz engelleme:** kapı kapalıysa hata mesajı tek ve aynı — "engellendin" ile "henüz bağlı değilsiniz" ayırt edilmiyor.

**Şikayet:** `sikayet_gonder`'in izin verdiği hedef türlerine `mesaj` ekleniyor; küçük bir migrasyon.

**Gerçek zamanlı:** Supabase Realtime, `mesajlar` tablosunun insert'lerine abonelik. Okuma RLS'i üyelik + engelleme kontrolü yaptığı için abonelik de aynı kuraldan geçiyor — ayrı bir filtre kurmuyorum, yoksa iki yerde iki kural olur ve ayrışır.

Bu bölüm doğru mu? Onaylarsan Bölüm 3'e (ekranlar ve test yaklaşımı) geçiyorum.

**Beş ekran dokunuşu:**

| Ekran | Değişiklik |
|---|---|
| `mesajlar` (yeni) | Mesaj kutusu: karşı kişi, son mesaj önizlemesi, okunmamış rozeti, satır başına "Gizle" |
| `sohbet/[kullaniciId]` (yeni) | Konuşma: mesaj listesi, yazma alanı, şikayet girişi |
| `index` | "Mesajlar" girişi + toplam okunmamış sayısı (mevcut "Baglar" rozetinin deseni) |
| `kullanici/[id]` | Kapı açıkken "Mesaj gönder" butonu |
| `baglar` | İki takip listesi tek listeye iniyor, tek "Bağı kopar" butonu |

Rotayı `kullaniciId` üzerinden kuruyorum, `konusmaId` üzerinden değil — böylece profilden, henüz hiç konuşma yokken de açılabiliyor; konuşmayı `mesaj_gonder` ilk mesajda oluşturuyor.

Kapı kapalıyken yazma alanının yerini kısa bir not alıyor ve geçmiş okunur kalıyor.

**Test yaklaşımı iki katmanlı, Faz 3a'daki gibi:**

Jest (mock'lu) istemci modülünü ve beş ekranı tutuyor. Ama asıl kanıt canlı veritabanı senaryolarında, çünkü **yazma kapısı bu fazın güvenlik yüzeyi** ve mock'lu test onu yapısal olarak ölçemiyor. Yeni senaryolar: kabulün iki satır yazması, koparmanın iki satırı da silmesi, karşılıklı takiplilerin yazışabilmesi, sohbet isteğiyle bağlananların takip olmadan yazışabilmesi, bağsız kişinin yazamaması, engellinin yazamaması ve engellemenin konuşmayı gizlemesi, bağ koptuktan sonra geçmişin okunup yazmanın reddedilmesi, iki yolun da **aynı** konuşmaya çıkması, gizlenen konuşmanın karşı taraf yazınca geri gelmesi, okunmamış sayısının doğruluğu, ve kimliksiz çağrıların reddi. Mevcut 19-28 senaryoları da karşılıklı takibe göre güncelleniyor.

**Bir şeyi bu faza dahil etmeyi öneriyorum:** görünürlük paketi zaten kendi kotasını tüketiyor — bugün iki kez elle temizlemek zorunda kaldım — ve bu faz istek gönderen senaryoların sayısını belirgin artırıyor. Kalıcı çözüm olmadan paket günde ~4-5 koşuma düşer ve yanlış alarm vermeye başlar, ki bu fazın kendi doğrulamasını çürütür. Senaryoların hesapları dönüşümlü kullanması ya da koşucunun kendi kotasını yönetici anahtarıyla temizlemesi — birini bu fazın içine alalım.

Doğrulama seti Faz 3a'daki gibi: `jest --runInBand`, `test:sema`, `test:gorunurluk`, `tsc --noEmit`.

Bu bölüm de onaylanırsa spec'i yazıp `docs/superpowers/specs/` altına commit'liyorum, sonra senin gözden geçirmen için duruyorum.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant

Function signatures match the brief exactly (`yoneticiIstemcisi(): SupabaseClient | null`, `kotayiTemizle(kimlikler: string[]): Promise&lt;void&gt;`), `temizle()` calls `kotayiTemizle` at its end after existing cleanup steps, commit message matches the brief's exact text, and only `mobil/gorunurluk-testleri/yardimcilar.ts` was listed as "Modify" but the implementer justified and documented the necessary `calistir.ts` addition (the brief's own field names `t.aId/bId/cId` don't exist in the real `Temizlenecekler` type — verified: `yardimcilar.ts:126-135` pre-diff had no id-bearing field, so wiring real ids required touching the caller).

- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether the `service_role` key was actually added to `mobil/.env` on the implementer's machine (it's gitignored, correctly absent from the diff) — not a defect, matches the reported "key absent" scenario the controller already accepted as out of scope for this task.

### Named-risk checks

1. **Secret handling** — Checked. `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is read only via `process.env` at `yardimcilar.ts:185`, passed straight into `createClient(URL, anahtar, ...)` (`yardimcilar.ts:187`), never interpolated into any log/error string. The only error logging touching the admin client is `console.error('  Kota temizligi basarisiz:', error.message)` (`yardimcilar.ts:216`), which logs the Postgres error message, not the key or client object. No new file writes anywhere in the diff. Confirmed `mobil/.env` is listed in `.gitignore:13` and `git ls-files` reports it untracked (matches report). No secret exposure found.

2. **Blast radius** — Checked. The only delete is `yonetici.from('istek_gunlugu').delete().in('gonderen_id', kimlikler)` (`yardimcilar.ts:211-214`) — always column-filtered, never a bare `.delete()`. Traced the id source: `t.hesapKimlikleri` is populated at `calistir.ts:109` (`t.hesapKimlikleri.push(aId, bId)`), immediately after `const { a, b, aId, bId } = await ikiKullaniciIleBaglan()` at `calistir.ts:107` — these ids come from `kullaniciIleBaglan`'s `data.user!.id` (yardimcilar.ts:38), a non-null asserted real auth id, not a guessable/empty placeholder. `cId` is pushed at `calistir.ts:696` right after `ucuncuKullaniciIleBaglan()` resolves. By the time `temizle(t)` runs at `calistir.ts:1004`, the array is populated by two synchronous pushes that ran to completion before any scenario code executed (scenario failures are caught inside `senaryo()` at `calistir.ts:99-103` and do not abort `main()`). Even in the theoretical case of an empty array, `.in('col', [])` is documented by the task as matching nothing in Supabase — no path constructs a filterless or all-matching delete.

3. **Graceful degradation** — Checked. `yoneticiIstemcisi()` returns `null` on missing key (`yardimcilar.ts:186`); `kotayiTemizle` checks for `null` and returns early after printing a multi-line, visually distinct `console.warn` block naming the exact env var, the failure mode ("~8 runs -&gt; false alarm"), and explicit remediation guidance ("check quota first, not the code") — `yardimcilar.ts:204-208`. Independently verified live: both foreground runs printed this exact warning and `istek_gunlugu` held 20 accumulated rows afterward, confirming the no-key path is real and was not silently faked as a successful clean. The warning is loud and specific enough to point a future reader at the quota rather than the code.

4. **Deviation from the brief** — Checked. Brief's guessed field names (`t.aId/bId/cId`) genuinely don't exist on `Temizlenecekler`; verified pre-diff shape at `yardimcilar.ts:126-129` (only `checkInler`, `engellemeler`, `takipler`). Adding `hesapKimlikleri: string[]` (`yardimcilar.ts:130-134`) and populating it via two `push` calls in `calistir.ts` (lines 109, 696) is the right shape — it mirrors the exact accumulator pattern already used for `checkInler`/`engellemeler`/`takipler` (populated via `push` from `calistir.ts`, consumed by `temizle()` in `yardimcilar.ts`), so it fits the file's existing structure rather than inventing a new one. Confirmed no scenario path leaves `hesapKimlikleri` empty when `temizle()` runs: population happens synchronously right after each connect call, before any scenario or other awaited call that could throw and abort `main()` early; if a connect itself failed, `main()` would abort before `t` is meaningfully used and `temizle()` would never be reached at all, so "empty list at temizle() time" is not a reachable state in the current control flow.

### Strengths
- Defensive coding is layered correctly: non-empty id list by construction, plus `.in()` semantics that are safe even on an empty list, plus a loud warning on the no-key path — three independent safeguards against the exact failure this task exists to prevent (accidental mass delete or silent no-op).
- New code fits the file's existing conventions: log-and-continue error handling (matches the other three cleanup loops in `temizle()`), function-declaration hoisting used cleanly so `kotayiTemizle` can be called before its own text position, no unnecessary new imports.
- Commit message and exported function signatures match the brief exactly.
- ASCII-only requirement independently verified: manually inspected every added line in the diff, all comments and strings use plain hyphens and ASCII apostrophes, no Turkish diacritics or em-dashes.
- Honest reporting: report explicitly flags that the two-run 82/0 result does not prove the key-present delete path works, rather than overclaiming.

1. **`found` ordering** — Verified in `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820123552_takip_karsilikli.sql:48-58`. `if not found then raise` immediately follows the `update`, before the mirror `insert`. The reject branch has its own independent `if not found then raise` at lines 66-68, right after its `delete`. No shared post-if/else check remains (the old shared check at the end of the if/else, present in `20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql:102-104`, was correctly removed and replaced by two per-branch checks). 2. **Transcription fidelity** — Diffed against `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260819190203_bag_rpcleri.sql:73-109` (the source it was copied from). Auth guard (`auth.uid() is null`) present and first statement in both new functions. Recipient-only condition `takip_edilen_id = auth.uid()` preserved in both the accept `update` (line 45) and the reject `delete` (line 63), with `takip_eden_id = p_kullanici_id` correctly identifying the original sender — a sender cannot accept their own request. `durum = 'beklemede'` filter preserved in both branches. Reject branch's delete is byte-for-byte identical to the original except one comment word (`silinir` -&gt; `siliniyor`, purely tense, no functional change). Confirmed faithful.

3. **Backfill correctness** — `where t.durum = 'kabul'` at line ~104 (only accepted rows are mirrored; pending rows untouched, matching the constraint that a pending request is not yet a bond). `on conflict (takip_eden_id, takip_edilen_id) do nothing` prevents overwrite; the primary key is exactly `(takip_eden_id, takip_edilen_id)` (`20260819183239_takipler.sql:11`), so the conflict target is valid. The `insert ... select` reads from the same table it writes to, but Postgres takes a single statement-start snapshot, so newly inserted rows are not re-selected within the same statement — no duplication risk, and idempotent under re-application. Confirmed correct.

4. **`takibi_birak` symmetry** — Lines 97-99: `(takip_eden_id = auth.uid() and takip_edilen_id = p_kullanici_id) or (takip_eden_id = p_kullanici_id and takip_edilen_id = auth.uid())`. Exactly the two orientations between the two named users, nothing broader (no wildcard, no third-party rows touched). Confirmed correct.

5. **Untouched blast radius** — Confirmed the diff touches only the one new migration file and nothing else. Checked the actual quota mechanism (superseded since this diff's base commit): `bag.istek_on_kontrol` in `20260819195646_istek_gunlugu.sql:51-54` counts `public.istek_gunlugu`, not `takipler`, and that table is populated only by `takip_istegi_gonder`/`sohbet_istegi_gonder` (`20260819195646_istek_gunlugu.sql:97,123`, later wrapped for double-tap safety in `20260820052327_istek_gonder_cift_dokunma.sql`). - Task 5 brief lists `mobil/supabase/migrations/&lt;timestamp&gt;_mesajlar.sql` plus (Step 3) `mobil/gorunurluk-testleri/sema-dogrula.ts` — both touched: `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820131833_mesajlar.sql` (new) and 62-line addition to `sema-dogrula.ts` (6 assertions).
- Three migration files, three commits (`a618334`, `e3809c6`, `e939830`), verified in diff header — matches brief-by-brief split.

### Named-risk checks
1. **Deliberate policy gap** — Confirmed. `20260820131703_konusmalar.sql:1-17` creates `konusmalar`, enables RLS, revokes write, and adds **no** policy. The `konusmalar` select policy ("konusma uyeligi") appears only in `20260820131753_konusma_uyeleri.sql:44-53`, alongside `konusma_uyeleri`'s own creation. No premature/"helpful" policy was added in task 3. Correct.
2. **`birebir_anahtar` uniqueness** — `20260820131703_konusmalar.sql:110`: `birebir_anahtar  text unique,` — no `not null`, so it is nullable and a unique constraint that ignores nulls, matching the stated need for future `mekan_odasi` rows. Correct.
3. **Message length constraint** — `20260820131833_mesajlar.sql:169`: `metin text not null check (length(trim(metin)) between 1 and 2000)`. Uses `trim()` before `length()`, so an all-whitespace string trims to length 0 and is rejected, and 2000 trimmed spaces-plus-one-char can't pass either. Correct, matches spec exactly (spec line 161 in design doc has the identical constraint).
4. **New schema assertions** — All six combinations present (`sema-dogrula.ts:394-432`): insert/update x {konusmalar, konusma_uyeleri, mesajlar}. Asserted code `'42501'` matches the report's observed probe output. Verified the probes use constraint-satisfying payloads (valid `tur`, existing `aId`) so the only possible rejection cause is the privilege/RLS layer, not an incidental FK/check failure — the assertions genuinely exercise what they claim. Reused the file's existing `code === 'X'` -&gt; `true` boolean-comparison idiom (same pattern at lines 35, 175, 184, 223, 268), not an ad hoc convention.
   - One nuance worth flagging (see Minor below): Postgres reports SQLSTATE `42501` for both "permission denied for table" (missing GRANT) and "new row violates row-level security policy" (RLS with no permissive policy) — same code either way. So these assertions verify that writes are blocked overall, but would not by themselves distinguish a regression that reverts only the `revoke` line (RLS would still block with the identical code) from one that reverts the whole write gate. Practically this doesn't understate risk — an actual bypass (both layers down) is still caught, since only then would the calls succeed and `error` would be `undefined`.
5. **Index usefulness** — `mesajlar_konusma_zaman on (konusma_id, olusturuldu desc)` (`20260820131833_mesajlar.sql:174-175`). Serves history pagination directly (equality on `konusma_id`, range/order on `olusturuldu`). Also serves "last message per conversation" lookups via a per-`konusma_id` `ORDER BY olusturuldu DESC LIMIT 1` (e.g. a LATERAL join from `konusma_uyeleri`/`konusmalar` for an inbox listing), which this composite index serves as an index scan without a sort. Column order is correct for both named use cases, and matches the spec's own index prescription (design doc line 161-162) verbatim.

- Migrations are copied essentially verbatim from the briefs — no scope creep, no drift between plan and implementation.
- The three-file/three-commit split is exactly as required, and the deliberate task-3/task-4 policy gap (Named Risk 1) is handled precisely as specified — a real discipline point, since it would have been easy to "helpfully" add the policy early and break the plan's ordering rationale.
- No direct subqueries against `takipler`, `engellemeler`, or `sohbet_istekleri`; `mesajlar`'s policy correctly reuses the pre-existing `gizli.engelli_mi` helper (in place since 2026-08-16), avoiding the known recursion failure mode.
- ASCII/BOM compliance verified independently (`grep -nP '[^\x00-\x7F]'` on all four changed files: no matches; hex-dumped file heads: no BOM).
- New assertions reuse the file's existing idiom rather than inventing a new one, and use constraint-safe payloads so the observed 42501 is unambiguously attributable to the privilege/RLS layer.
- No already-applied migration was edited; all three are new files.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `sema-dogrula.ts:394-432` — the six new assertions check only for SQLSTATE `42501`, which Postgres returns identically for "table grant revoked" and "RLS blocks with no permissive policy." A comment noting this overlap (i.e., that the assertions verify the externally observable write-block, not specifically that the `revoke` statement is in effect) would make the test's actual coverage clearer to future readers, though no fix to behavior is needed.

**Önbellekleme yok, `immutable` işaretlemesi yok.** Kapı her mesajda çağrılıyor, konuşma açılırken bir kez değil. Faz 3a'daki iki gerçek kusur tam olarak bunun tersinden çıkmıştı: kural girişte kontrol ediliyor, sonra durum değişince delinmiş oluyordu. Biri yabancıların GPS konumunu okutuyordu.

Bir de yeni iddia konusunda serbest bıraktım ama şartlı: ucuz ve dürüst bir şey bulursa eklesin, bulamazsa **uydurmasın** ve hangisini yaptığını söylesin. Kapının gerçek kanıtı zaten Görev 17'de, iki gerçek kullanıcının birbirine yazmayı denediği yerde gelecek.

<result>Confirmed — precedent match is accurate, and even the comment wording is copied verbatim. All checks complete. Writing the final report.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant: gate signature `bag.yazabilir_mi(p_hedef uuid) returns boolean`, block precedence, mutual-follow-or-accepted-request logic, `stable`, schema `bag`, and the sema-dogrula assertion all match brief `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-20-faz3b-birebir-sohbet/task-6-brief.md` line-for-line (report's claim of "aynen kopyalandi" verified against `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820132747_yazabilir_mi.sql:23-55` vs brief lines 23-56 — identical).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: end-to-end behavioral correctness of the composed gate (block/follow/request interplay) against a live database is not exercised by anything in this diff — the report and the task brief both say that's deferred to a later task, and the controller's own live-DB verification (outside this diff) is what actually establishes it. Judging the diff alone, correctness rests on manual reading of the SQL, which checks out (see risk-by-risk below).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether any call site elsewhere in the codebase might cache/memoize `yazabilir_mi`'s result — no call site exists yet in this diff (Task 6 is gate-only), so there is nothing to check for memoization within scope.

### Named-risk checks
1. **Both-directions follow check** — `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820132747_yazabilir_mi.sql:40-42`: `bag.takip_ediyor_mu(auth.uid(), p_hedef) and bag.takip_ediyor_mu(p_hedef, auth.uid())`. Combined with `and`, not `or`. Correct — a one-directional row cannot satisfy this branch.
2. **Block precedence** — `yazabilir_mi.sql:30-52`: the function body is `select not gizli.engelli_mi(p_hedef) and ( &lt;follow-or-request&gt; )`. The negated block check is the left operand of the outer `and`, gating the entire parenthesized OR expression, not sitting inside one branch of it. Correct — a block short-circuits both the follow path and the request path.
3. **Volatility/caching** — `yazabilir_mi.sql:27`: declared `stable` (not `immutable`). Correct per decision 45 (re-evaluated per call, not cached at conversation-open time). No memoization introduced in this diff; no call site exists yet to audit for client-side caching (out of Task 6's scope).
4. **Schema placement** — `yazabilir_mi.sql:23`: `create or replace function bag.yazabilir_mi(...)`. Confirmed in `bag`, not `public`.
5. **The new assertion** — `mobil/gorunurluk-testleri/sema-dogrula.ts:425-431`: calls `a.rpc('yazabilir_mi', { p_hedef: bId })` (unqualified name, which PostgREST resolves only against the exposed `public` schema) and asserts `error !== null`. Verified this is not a vacuous check: `a` is a real authenticated client and `bId` a real user id from `ikiKullaniciIleBaglan()` (`sema-dogrula.ts:6`), the same pair used successfully elsewhere in the file. If the function were moved to `public`, PostgREST would resolve and execute it (new Postgres functions get implicit `PUBLIC EXECUTE` by default, and neither this migration nor its two named precedents revoke it), so the call would succeed and `error` would be `null` — the assertion would then correctly fail. The assertion is an exact structural copy of the pre-existing `bag.takip_ediyor_mu` precedent (`sema-dogrula.ts:186-193`), including the same explanatory comment. Judged: does test what it claims.

### The PUBLIC EXECUTE question
**Severity: Minor (not blocking).** Reasoning:
- The migration's `grant execute on function bag.yazabilir_mi(uuid) to authenticated;` (`yazabilir_mi.sql:55`) never revokes from `public`/`anon`, so the deployed ACL leaves PUBLIC's implicit default EXECUTE grant in place, exactly as the controller found.
- This is not a new deviation introduced by this task: it is byte-for-byte what the approved brief specified (`task-6-brief.md:55`, no revoke line), and it exactly matches the shape of the two functions this task was explicitly told to mirror — `bag.takip_ediyor_mu` (`20260819184102_bag_takip_ediyor_mu.sql:26-27`) and `gizli.engelli_mi` (`20260816140000_gizli_engelli_mi_fonksiyonu.sql:17-18`) — neither of which revokes from public/anon either. `bag.istek_on_kontrol` from the prior phase is the same pattern again. So the "project convention" of revoke-then-grant, as actually practiced in this codebase, applies to `public`-schema client-facing RPCs (every one of the ~30 `revoke execute on function public.*` lines found), not to `bag`/`gizli` private helpers — those instead rely on schema-level `usage` being withheld from `public`/`anon` (`grant usage on schema bag to authenticated;`, `20260819184102_bag_takip_ediyor_mu.sql:26`, not repeated/contradicted anywhere). Without `USAGE` on schema `bag`, PUBLIC cannot resolve `bag.yazabilir_mi(...)` at all in ordinary SQL, and PostgREST does not expose the `bag` schema as an API surface regardless of function-level grants — both facts the controller independently verified.
- Real-world exploitability today is effectively nil: it requires either (a) `bag` being added to PostgREST's exposed-schema list, or (b) some future code/role explicitly granted `USAGE` on schema `bag` alongside default execute rights. Neither is present.
- It is nonetheless a cheap, worthwhile hardening gap on the single highest-value function in the phase — a defense-in-depth belt that costs one line and removes reliance on schema-usage scoping as the only backstop. I'd flag it for a follow-up cleanup migration across all three `bag`/`gizli` security-definer helpers (including the two precedents), not as a blocker on this task, since this task did exactly what its approved brief and its named precedents told it to do.

### Strengths
- Migration is a verbatim, faithful implementation of the reviewed/approved brief SQL — zero unrequested deviation.
- Comments explain *why*, not just *what*: the double-follow-check comment (`yazabilir_mi.sql:34-38`) explicitly walks through the "kabul already writes both rows, so a single check would normally suffice — but a stray one-directional row from bad data or a future bug would silently read as connected" reasoning. This is exactly the kind of non-obvious rationale a future reader needs, and it correctly frames the check as tolerating a *data* anomaly without ever escalating it into a *security* one.
- The header comment (`yazabilir_mi.sql:18-22`) states the "called every message, not once at conversation-open" invariant and ties it to decision 45 by number, plus the "why bag not public" PostgREST-exposure rationale — both load-bearing facts for a future reader who might otherwise "optimize" by caching the result or moving the function.
- New test assertion is added in the same style/location as the established precedent, keeps the running count internally consistent (59→60, independently grep-verified by the implementer), and is genuinely load-bearing (would fail on a `public`-schema regression).
- ASCII-only constraint respected (grep-verified by implementer, spot-checked by me — file reads clean).
- Uses an existing migration-timestamp-new-file convention; no edits to any already-applied migration.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260820132747_yazabilir_mi.sql:55` — add `revoke execute on function bag.yazabilir_mi(uuid) from public, anon;` before the grant, for defense-in-depth consistency with the public-RPC convention, even though current schema-usage scoping already makes it unreachable. Best done as a single follow-up migration that also retrofits `bag.takip_ediyor_mu`, `gizli.engelli_mi`, and `bag.istek_on_kontrol`, since fixing only the newest function while leaving its two named precedents inconsistent would look like an unexplained style deviation to a future reader.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The gate correctly implements decisions 43/45 — both-directions follow check combined with `and`, block precedence gating the full expression, `stable` volatility, correct schema placement — verified both by source reading and by the controller's independent live-database behavioral matrix (5/5 correct, including the discriminating one-directional-follow and block-precedence cases). The one flagged item (missing `revoke ... from public, anon`) is a pre-existing, low-exploitability pattern inherited verbatim from the approved brief and its two named precedent functions, not a defect introduced by this task.</result>

This matches the brief's SQL verbatim, and cross-checks against the spec (decisions 43-45) and sibling migrations (`konusmalar`, `konusma_uyeleri`, `mesajlar`, `bag.yazabilir_mi`) all hold up. I now have everything needed to write the report.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant. Signature matches `public.mesaj_gonder(p_kullanici_id uuid, p_metin text) returns uuid` (spec line 197, migration line 4-7). Gate re-checked on every send, not cached at conversation-open time (decision 45, migration line 34). Both members' `gizlendi_mi` cleared and only sender's `son_okuma` advanced (decision 44/46, migration lines 68-74). Error strings match the spec's error table exactly: "Kimlik dogrulamasi gerekli" (line 17 vs spec line 261), "Mesaj cok uzun" (line 29 vs spec line 260), "Bu kisiye su an mesaj gonderemezsin" (line 35 vs spec line 258, one message for both block and no-bond causes). All four global conventions present: `set search_path = public` (line 10), `auth.uid() is null` as the first statement (line 16), `revoke ... from public, anon` + `grant ... to authenticated` (lines 80-81). ASCII-only confirmed by `grep -nP '[^\x00-\x7F]'` on both changed files (no matches), no BOM (`xxd` on first 3 bytes shows `2d 2d 20`, i.e. `-- `), no em-dash (only plain ASCII `--`). Timestamp `20260820133817` sorts after the three table migrations and `yazabilir_mi` (131703/131753/131833/132747), so apply order is correct.

### Named-risk checks
1. **Silent blocking** — verified. Exactly one `raise exception` on the gate-failure path (`mesaj_gonder.sql:34-36`), fed by a single boolean from `bag.yazabilir_mi`, which itself unions the block-check and both bond-check branches into one `select` (`20260820132747_yazabilir_mi.sql:13-35`). No branch in `mesaj_gonder` distinguishes "blocked" from "not yet bonded" — confirmed no second raise/message exists on that path.

2. **`istek_gunlugu` untouched** — verified. Grepped the full migration file; zero occurrences of `istek_gunlugu`. The function only touches `konusmalar`, `konusma_uyeleri`, `mesajlar` (writes) and reads `bag.yazabilir_mi`.

3. **Race in find-or-create** — verified, reasoning holds. `insert ... on conflict (birebir_anahtar) do nothing returning id into v_konusma_id` (lines 48-51): plpgsql's non-strict `INTO` sets the variable to `null` when `DO NOTHING` suppresses the row, which is exactly what happens for the losing side of a concurrent insert. Postgres's `ON CONFLICT` speculative-insertion protocol serializes the two inserts (the loser blocks until the winner's transaction ends), so by the time the loser's `do nothing` returns, the winner's row is already committed and visible to the loser's re-select (lines 54-58) under read committed. Sound.

4. **Member insert placement** — verified, no gap found. The `insert into konusma_uyeleri` (lines 60-62) sits inside the *outer* `if v_konusma_id is null then` block (opened at line 47), which is entered whenever the initial lookup (lines 43-45) found no existing conversation — true for both the race winner and the race loser, not just the winner. Both branches therefore always attempt to insert both member rows, with `on conflict do nothing` absorbing the duplicate from whichever side loses. Since `konusmalar` rows can only be created by this same function (RLS has no insert policy, confirmed in `20260820131703_konusmalar.sql:17`), and both the conversation insert and member insert live in the same transaction, there is no path that leaves a conversation with fewer than two members.

5. **Validation ordering** — verified. Order is: auth check (16-18) -&gt; self-message check (20-22) -&gt; empty check (24-26) -&gt; length check (28-30) -&gt; gate check (34-36) -&gt; write logic. All content validation precedes the gate, so a malformed message never reaches `bag.yazabilir_mi` or the tables. Stored value is `trim(p_metin)` (line 66), not the raw parameter — matches the table-level check `length(trim(metin)) between 1 and 2000` on `mesajlar` (`20260820131833_mesajlar.sql:5`), so a message with only leading/trailing whitespace beyond the trimmed content is stored trimmed, consistent with the check constraint.

### Cases my test may have missed
None. I traced the two update-target columns (`gizlendi_mi`, `son_okuma`) and the realtime-relevant `mesajlar` insert against the schema and RLS policies in the sibling migrations, and found nothing your seven-behaviour walkthrough didn't already exercise. The one behavior the source *doesn't* test end-to-end — the find-or-create race itself — can't be exercised by a single-session manual walkthrough anyway; it needed the code-level reasoning done above, not a runtime probe.

### Strengths
- The migration is a byte-for-byte match of the reviewed, decision-backed brief SQL — no drift introduced during implementation.
- Comments consistently explain *why*, not just *what*: the race-safety comment (line 53), the single-message rationale tied explicitly to "Faz 2b sessizlik ilkesi" (lines 32-33), and the gizlilik/son_okuma rationale tied to decision 44 (lines 68-70).
- The implementer added a cheap, correctly-scoped defensive test (anon-caller 42501 check) that validates this exact task's revoke/grant wiring without touching `istek_gunlugu` or writing live rows — consistent with the report's stated reason for not exercising the write path in this task.
- Table-level check constraint (`mesajlar.metin`) backs up the RPC's own length/emptiness validation — defense in depth, not redundant risk.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `mesaj_gonder.sql:71-74` — the `update konusma_uyeleri` runs unconditionally on every single message, touching both member rows even when neither `gizlendi_mi` was true nor the sender's `son_okuma` needs to move forward beyond a microsecond. This is correct, not a bug, but it means every message send emits two `UPDATE` events (in addition to the `INSERT`) to any Realtime subscriber on `konusma_uyeleri`. A guard like `where konusma_id = v_konusma_id and (gizlendi_mi or kullanici_id = auth.uid())` would cut this to one write in the steady state. Not worth blocking on given this table's low realtime fan-out (per-conversation membership, not a hot broadcast channel).

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The migration is an exact, verified reproduction of a spec-backed brief; every named risk (silent blocking, `istek_gunlugu` isolation, race convergence, member-insert completeness, validation ordering/trimming) traces cleanly through the actual applied SQL with no gaps, and all global conventions (ASCII, no-BOM, auth check ordering, revoke/grant) are satisfied.</result>
